> **Bounded provider-envelope checkpoint 2026-07-31 (counts unchanged;
> `GOLD-R4-15k1` remains open):** the production
> OpenAI/OpenRouter/DeepSeek/Kimi/Qwen/custom Chat-Completions path now reads
> successful JSON incrementally into an 8 MiB hard-capped byte envelope before
> deserialisation. SSE framing is byte-oriented with a 1 MiB line/residual and
> joined-event-data cap, so split UTF-8 code points and standard multi-`data:`
> events work without permitting newline-free or multi-field growth. Retained
> refusal metadata has its own 1 MiB cumulative budget across streaming,
> `message.refusal` and `content[].refusal`; every oversize, invalid-UTF-8,
> malformed or truncated-transport outcome exposes only domain-separated digest
> evidence. Typed QuotaError,
> Retry-After, OpenRouter observation and ordinary visible provider refusal
> output remain intact. Shared primitives live in
> `providers/response_bounds.rs`; focused evidence is response-bounds **3/3**,
> OpenAI-compatible **53/53**, redaction **53/53**, quota **22/22**,
> termination **4/4**, refusal recovery **26/26**, abliterated fallback
> **11/11** and turn journal **13/13**, with Cargo check, Clippy
> `-D warnings`, ROAD release gate **11/11** and lost-feature integrity
> **19/19** green. `GOLD-R4-15k1` cannot close until Ollama, Anthropic,
> Gemini, Cohere, Bedrock, Azure, Copilot-token, Claude CLI/Tmux and
> RecursiveMAS adopt equivalent bounded readers and no-bypass tests. Current
> machine truth remains **1,222 total / 988 complete / 234 raw open / 233
> pre-tag blockers**.

> **Mutation-WAL integrity checkpoint 2026-07-28 (counts unchanged; R3-17
> remains open):** CLI/GUI install, replace, create and remove plus proactive
> extraction, Curator, Teacher and Self-Improve now use one capability-bound,
> crash-recoverable mutation lifecycle. Exact package/target generations,
> authenticated pre-rename intents, correlated terminal receipts and
> restart reconciliation cover the public mutation boundary; absent uninstall
> produces no false operation. An indeterminate removal restores only its exact
> operation-bound tombstone after object-identity and v2-generation proof;
> missing, drifted or ambiguous evidence stays blocked. Package generations
> include paths, kinds, bytes and permission semantics, recursive copies
> preserve directory/file modes, and home-WAL/key enumeration is bounded before
> filtering as well as by aligned directory, segment, archive, byte and
> decompression limits. Fresh HMAC-key creation durably commits the key's
> directory entry. Lock waits, reconciliation, WAL/key scans and tree I/O run
> outside Tokio workers, including a single-worker starvation regression.
> Daemon endpoint discovery invalidates its tuple before releasing ownership
> and accepts only an unchanged PID/nonce snapshot sandwiched by live OS-lock
> proofs. This does not implement the still-missing installed-Skill authority
> record, independent current anchor or activation receipt, and same-user OS
> IPC remains a release hardening remainder. Two independent adversarial
> full-slice reviews report zero Critical/High/Medium/Low findings after the
> fixes. Deferred Cargo/runtime gates and exact-head CI/Security/CodeQL evidence
> are still pending; current dashboard counts remain **WS-R3 19 total / 13 done
> / 6 open** and **WS-R4 14 total / 1 done / 13 open**; no R3/R4 box is closed.
>

> **Dream reload/commit plus GUI onboarding checkpoint 2026-07-28
> (`3365f2f0` plus this GUI landing; counts unchanged,
> `GOLD-LF-P2-23` remains open only for consolidated integration/release
> gates):** `dream.cron_enabled` now drives the real
> scheduler and operator readback. Each accepted config is published together
> with its epoch and generation-bound commit gate as one atomic snapshot.
> Reload/shutdown retire and drain the old generation before replacement or
> cancellation; every irreversible provider, persistence, Obsidian,
> self-improve, queue and WAL leaf holds the corresponding lease through the
> actual effect. `Strict`, `Custom` and explicit disable fail closed at both
> fleet admission and effect commit. The fleet owns and joins the real task,
> shutdown retirement is permanent, and even unrelated accepted reloads replace
> a still-desired or just-finished Dream with the exact new snapshot.
> Deterministic race/effect tests, file-scoped rustfmt/diff checks and an
> independent final review passed with no HIGH/MEDIUM finding. The GUI wizard
> and Settings are wired to the typed CLI receipt plus verified status readback,
> never to a replacement config mutation. Canonical false wins legacy true;
> malformed values fail closed; Settings is command-only; revision/operation
> ordering rejects stale callbacks. Only verified Rust completion may navigate
> out of Finish, while malformed, partial and failed completion retains the
> visible retry surface. Source/UI regressions cover the complete contract. A
> separate re-review found the former unconditional Slint navigation as HIGH
> and approved its root-cause closure at 0 HIGH/0 MEDIUM. P2-23 now remains open
> only until the deferred consolidated Cargo/runtime/release matrix and
> exact-head gates execute the completed runtime and GUI wiring.

> **Request-bound GUI/Buddy stream lifecycle checkpoint 2026-07-28 (counts
> unchanged; `GOLD-LF-P2-26` remains open):** the provider subprocess now emits
> an authenticated provider-complete frame before post-reply pipelines and an
> authenticated final frame only after they succeed. A pure request reducer
> owns Waiting/Receiving/Finalizing and terminal state, cancellation, exact
> surface identity and a one-shot provider-dispatch claim. Main Chat and Buddy
> consume the same canonical conversation rows, child supervisor and phase
> projection; queued callbacks revalidate identity/cancellation/phase at UI
> delivery, model overrides and private consent tokens are request-bound, and
> minimizing/restoring or opening retained history mid-stream retains Stop and
> visible progress. Provider launch is now a private, bounded stdin envelope:
> the child blocks on it before config/provider/hooks/tools, the subprocess is
> supervised before an atomic request gate can commit, and Stop winning that
> gate prevents the envelope and therefore provider egress. The authenticated
> stream nonce is no longer inherited through the environment. A failed process
> kill remains supervised, watchdog-active and explicitly retryable instead of
> being reported as stopped. Provider routes can enter turn orchestration only
> through one typed framing seam. Windows parks the child in a kill-on-close Job
> before launch commit. Linux starts a manager-owned transient systemd service
> (`Delegate=no`, `KillMode=control-group`) whose private guardian validates the
> GUI identity, hides the user D-Bus/runtime directory, cgroup ancestors and
> host process namespace, and treats missing trusted manager or namespace
> capabilities as a machine-readable fail-closed launch error; no process-group
> fallback remains. Provisioned Linux qualification makes missing containment
> capabilities fatal instead of silently skipping the adversarial
> crash/double-fork/escape suite. Other unsupported Unix targets remain
> explicitly fail closed. Executable tests cover stream/non-stream,
> composed/direct routing and write failure. Completed background jobs receive
> an atomic, exact stable-id transcript receipt before an authenticated typed
> notice may claim `durable=true`, materialise idempotently in the canonical
> conversation, restore from that receipt after restart, and only then commit
> the filesystem delivery marker. Incognito neither creates nor claims durable
> background work; DB/frame/UI/ACK crash boundaries remain recoverable. Split
> control lines never render as assistant text, a 16 MiB notice is decoded once
> instead of quadratically rescanned, and aggregate GUI stdout is bounded.
> Reduced Motion
> is static and the three phases expose
> progress semantics to assistive technology. Real transport reconnect/resume is
> not implemented: retry and auto-continue intentionally create a new request,
> so P2-26 cannot close. File-scoped rustfmt/static wiring/diff checks and
> independent code/security/Slint reviews are the construction gate; the
> consolidated Cargo/Slint/release matrix remains deferred until construction
> is complete.
>

> **Typed consumer-adoption checkpoint 2026-07-26 (R3-14 remains OPEN;
> counts unchanged):** MCP catalogue assembly now returns a dedicated typed
> value: trusted invocation instructions stay separate from one bounded
> `McpCatalogue` data envelope across Chat, Channels, sub-agent rebuilds and
> Cron. The protocol and its envelope share a semantic atomic budget group, so
> degradation retains both or removes both. Both public budget enforcers
> validate the exact one-A/one-D group shape before any mutation and return an
> error with the input unchanged for grouped B/C/E/Conductor, duplicate members
> or A-only/D-only orphans. Tool identifiers, property counts, required sets and schema summaries
> are bounded before catalogue allocation.
> Server IDs match the parser contract, allowlists take precedence exactly as
> at invocation, and duplicate IDs fail closed both on load and before an
> update is persisted. The bounded catalogue is a discovery view rather than
> an authorization grant/deny-list: omission through smart loading or caps
> changes visibility only; complete operator policy plus live gates remain
> authoritative. Server/byte totals are capped pre-envelope. One shared
> six-second deadline covers the bounded four-wide fetch rather than
> accumulating per-server timeouts; completed servers retain deterministic
> order and an unfinished batch carries an explicit `PARTIAL` marker. CLI and
> Channels resolve Council, MCP, refinement and direct routing once before
> catalogue I/O. Only the exact `McpDispatch` route may fetch/inject the
> catalogue; streaming, Council, direct and protocol-free refinement routes
> cannot inherit it. A typed loop-route trigger also preserves matched
> `loop: true` skills through CLI preflight: ForcedOff/AutoOff MCP runs the same
> protocol-free, minimum-two-round refinement path as Channels instead of
> silently degrading to one direct call.
> Repo-map context plus runtime `.neothhints`/`AGENTS.md` are canonical
> `RepoHint` data. Hint files are bounded before read/allocation; model/tool
> path bytes, components and command tokens are capped; canonical paths own
> dedup/accounting through lossless OS-byte SHA-256 identity; failed paths
> consume no successful-load budget; temporary drain pressure requeues pending
> leaves; complete envelopes have per-drain plus per-session wire ceilings; and
> filesystem work is offloaded from Tokio workers. `HINT_LOADED` separates
> source, included-payload and exact-wire provenance without storing body bytes.
> A static adapter treats convention claims as scoped evidence
> without granting tool, permission or policy authority. Skill `$ARGUMENTS` and
> slash `{args}` remain exclusively in the current user message rather than
> being copied into System/B authority.
> MCP dispatch-loop model replies are raw only for tool parsing and final
> display; its leaked-call retry, Goal/Grind continuation, goal judging and
> follow-ups use
> `ModelOutput` envelopes, and the judge now accepts only an exact `YES`.
> Goal audit distinguishes a real `not_met` from unavailable/budget failures,
> preserves terminal `budget_exhausted` across every terminal direct
> iteration/tool-call exit even after the one-shot goal nudge, and keeps a
> negative post-nudge judgement working to the cap. Multi-round records carry
> the exact aggregate outcome and original untruncated goal hash; only a final
> outer cap/budget exit becomes terminal, historical inner caps cannot emit a
> contradictory terminal event, and an inner exhausted Goal vetoes accidental
> outer convergence until a later `Met` or the real outer cap. Confirmed
> responses bypass post-judge refinement so the returned bytes are the bytes
> that were judged. Hash divergence, an incomplete judge prompt, and
> all-dispatches-failed Goal runs are typed non-bypassable errors; the latter two
> emit exactly one full-hash `input_budget_exceeded`/`unavailable` lifecycle
> frame before any fallback. Judge-disabled oversized Goals retain the legacy
> one-shot bounded nudge, while a judge-enabled oversized Goal is rejected before
> the first paid provider leaf. The outer Channel-Council caller cannot convert
> a dissent-loop integrity failure into a direct-provider fallback. A confirmed
> Goal and explicit `--until` criteria are conjunctive, and UUIDv7 loop IDs avoid
> concurrent WAL/record collisions. If a rejected structural gate causes a
> later round to replace the judged response, the earlier `Met` is cleared so
> only the actual final bytes can carry that verdict. CLI plus Channels use the same terminal WAL
> consumer. Untrusted D
> blocks cannot suppress either trusted Block-B discipline or clarification
> protocol.
> A focused production source tripwire pins the known removed raw signatures;
> typed APIs and behavior tests enforce the semantic boundary. Remaining R3-14
> work is attachments, recall/guidance and other memory, coding/compaction,
> remaining sub-agent/channel/Buddy consumers and every provider/retry/
> fallback/streaming/cost leaf.
>
> **Typed attachment/media checkpoint 2026-07-27 (R3-14 remains OPEN;
> counts unchanged):** `neoth chat --attach` now keeps the exact operator
> caption in Block E and carries every attachment as a canonical bounded Block-D
> envelope through the main, sub-agent, detached-background and TOML
> provider-backed slash paths. `/research` rejects attachments before starting
> its independent provider loop instead of silently ignoring them.
> No-follow single-handle admission runs on the blocking pool, rejects
> Unix FIFO/device blocking and Windows reparse points, enforces regular-file,
> count, per-kind and aggregate byte limits and rejects repeated paths plus
> byte-identical aliases. The read path reserves memory fallibly and charges the
> aggregate source budget incrementally, before retaining decoded content.
> Size/mtime drift is detected; same-size rewrites that preserve timestamps are
> not claimed as detected. Failures are operator errors
> rather than synthetic prompt text. Filename, kind and content stay atomic,
> exact provider separators count toward the aggregate wire limit, and
> required-retention fails an impossible provider budget rather than silently
> dropping an attachment. `/research` and local action slash commands reject
> before decode; extraction starts only after the turn WAL exists and drains it
> on failure. Cloud STT chat attachments fail before egress until a concrete
> request-bound cost/consent/WAL permit exists. Image, PDF, document/archive,
> audio and video decoders have input/expansion/output ceilings. Private
> tempfiles plus owned, detached lifecycle supervisors contain Docling, PDF,
> Faster-Whisper and video subprocess trees through success, failure, timeout
> and cancellation. Windows children are Job-bound; Linux/macOS process-group
> identity is proved before tree termination, and an unprovable cleanup poisons
> the relevant singleton permit instead of pretending capacity is free.
> Docling is bound to the effective media config, snapshots no-follow inputs and
> parses JSON with bounded page/text allocation. Web fetch streams into a hard
> response ceiling, rejects credential-bearing URLs, and writes its private
> cache atomically. Channel media now checks size before cloning, identifies PDF
> by MIME and uses the same config-bound backend chain. Source/behavior gates
> pin the removed raw prepend path. Consolidated local evidence: attachment unit
> **22/22**, attachment boundary **2/2**, CLI source gate **6/6**, Channel
> attachment source gate **17/17**, media-resource source gate **6/6**, PDF
> isolation **6/6**, Council adversarial **8/8**, serial Media **446/446**
> (two ffmpeg-dependent tests explicitly ignored), native OMI ingest **22/22**,
> Web Fetch **52/52**, Web Document Cache **9/9**, workspace rustfmt,

> request-authorized cloud media for chat attachments, Channel
> downloads/delegation, GUI/Buddy progress and retry, image OCR/vision text and
> the remaining memory/provider consumers.

> **Credential publication boundary correction 2026-07-28 (counts
> unchanged):** final security review found that `credential migrate --to
> keychain` restored the prior keychain generation on every file-commit error,
> including errors after the complete target pair was already published. That
> could leave `freedom.yaml` selecting keychain and `credentials.yaml` cleared
> while the new keychain value had been deleted. The CLI now shares the
> `DualFileTargetPublicationCrossed` boundary already used by OMI: only
> pre-boundary failures roll back; post-publication errors retain the new
> generation and recover the PREPARED journal. Exact fault-injection evidence
> covers `JournalPrepared`, `FreedomPublished` and the post-directory-sync
> window, including effective secret recovery and no-loss assertions.
>
> **Legacy SSH authority correction 2026-07-28 (counts unchanged):** final
> Rust review found that a temporary `open_store()` failure could demote an
> existing keychain SSH authority: the old public block was copied into
> `credentials.yaml`, which would then permanently shadow the keychain. The
> migration now requires a successful keychain open plus a successful compound

> **Test-isolation (2026-06-28):** fixed 3 parallel-suite-only `neothd --lib` flakes (green in isolation / under `--test-threads=1`, intermittently red under full parallel) — `daemon::pidfile::default_pidfile_lives_under_neoth_home` now sets+restores its OWN `NEOTH_HOME` under `test_env::lock()` (was reading ambient global, raced by `cron::runner`'s drop-lock-across-await); `cli::obsidian_wiki_rebuild_task::one_tick_rebuilds_wiki_pages_in_vault` now drives `run_one_tick()` to completion instead of `spawn()`+wait-for-index+`abort()` (the abort cancelled the task before the 0xFA WAL frame); `mcp::dispatch_loop::active_lease_lifts_dangerous_block_and_audits` green via a transient-I/O retry in `LeaseStore::load`/`save` (Windows AV sharing-violation on `leases.json` → fail-closed lift; also hardens prod). Full parallel `cargo test -p neothd --lib` green ×20 consecutive; `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean.
>
> **Follow-up (2026-06-29):** the `LeaseStore` transient-FS retry above (~50ms) was still too short — after sync to the heavier current suite, `mcp::dispatch_loop::active_lease_lifts_dangerous_block_and_audits` flaked ~1/12 again. Captured the exact branch via `#[cfg(test)]` DIAG markers: the risk-confirm single-use consume's `rename(leases.json.tmp → leases.json)` hits a Windows AV/indexer sharing-violation that outlasts 50ms → `consume_risk_leases` fail-closes → no `RISK_CONFIRM_USED` frame → `verdict=""`. Widened `retry_transient_io` to ~2s (20 attempts, linear backoff capped 150ms); fail-closed preserved, Unix no-op. Verified: dispatch green ×16 consecutive direct test-exe runs (it failed on run 1 pre-widen). Windows-local FS flake (Unix-CI parallel + Windows `--test-threads=1` CI don't hit it); also hardens prod — a valid risk-confirm lease no longer fails-closed on an AV hiccup.
>
> **Follow-up 2 (2026-06-29):** a 4th flake surfaced in the same hunt — `cluster::registry::tests::concurrent_{distinct_peer_upserts,rtt_and_stability_refresh}`. Root cause was lock-ordering, not FS: every registry write path took the OS file lock (`cluster.yaml.lock`) BEFORE the in-process `REGISTRY_LOCK` mutex, so concurrent same-process writers SPIN on the file lock (50ms retry, 5s give-up) instead of PARKING on the mutex — under full-suite CPU saturation the holder starves and the spinners trip the 5s timeout → `upsert`/`refresh` Err → test panic (`held by another process for >5s`). Fixed: swapped all 5 write paths to **mutex-first** (park don't spin; only the mutex-holder touches the file lock; cross-process lost-update protection preserved; consistent order = no deadlock). Windows-local/overload flake; correct structure regardless.

### v0.3 — Credentials import (from A4)

- [x] **C-01** `credentials/mod.rs` + `types.rs` — `CredentialImporter` trait + `ImportedCredential` with SecretString+mlock+zeroize. No on-disk plaintext copy. — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25 (hardened). Initial primitive shipped Session 24; Session 25 closed the three SecretBytes weaknesses Alex flagged in the v0.3 review. (1) **`Drop` now uses `zeroize::Zeroize`** (volatile writes + `compiler_fence(SeqCst)`), replacing the prior plain `*b = 0` loop that the compiler is free to elide. The `zeroize` crate was already a workspace dep (line 102 Cargo.toml). (2) **Automatic `Clone` removed** — duplicating secret material now goes through the explicit `SecretBytes::clone_for_storage(&self) -> Self` method. The name is intentionally grep-friendly: every site that fans out a secret shows up under `rg clone_for_storage` so a reviewer can audit them without a graph crawl. `ImportedCredential::Clone` is now hand-rolled (not derived) and forwards to `clone_for_storage`. (3) **`CredentialEntry.secret: String` field removed** entirely from `security::credential_redact` — the SC-17 redactor never read it, so carrying it through allocated a transient plaintext `String` on the way to /dev/null. `build_import_record` in `credentials/mod.rs` no longer calls `.expose_str().to_string()` on the SecretBytes; the secret stays inside the wizard's `ImportedCredential` and goes directly to the secret-store writer without a parallel allocation. Drift guard added (`credential_entry_carries_no_secret_field`) — a future PR that re-adds `secret: String` to `CredentialEntry` will fail this compile-time check via the explicit struct literal. Two existing tests updated for the new contract: `secret_bytes_clone_for_storage_equals_original_constant_time` (was `_clone_`) + `redactor_drops_name_url_username` (was `_secret`).

The original plan-text said "accepts encrypted export JSON" — that was overscoped for v0.3 and the unencrypted path is what operators actually export from the Bitwarden web vault. **Encrypted (password-protected) variant moved to C-02b** to keep the plan truthful. — 2026-05-26 Session 24 (parser) + Session 25 (plan-truth correction).

Algorithm correction: Bitwarden uses **AES-256-CBC + HMAC-SHA256** not AES-GCM (the plan-text was wrong), with **PBKDF2-SHA256 → HKDF-Expand-SHA256** two-step KDF. New `credentials::bitwarden_encrypted` module: `EncryptedExportEnvelope` typed shape + `BitwardenEncryptedError` taxonomy (8 variants — JsonParse / NotEncrypted / AccountKeyNotSupported / UnsupportedKdf / IterationsTooLow / EncryptedStringFormat / Base64 / WrongPassword single collapsed variant matching C-04b + C-03b side-channel policy) + `parse_envelope_json(body)` strict-shape validator (rejects non-encrypted / non-password-protected / Argon2id / iters < 100_000) + `derive_keys_pbkdf2(password, salt, iterations)` two-step KDF returning (enc_key, mac_key) + `parse_encrypted_string('2.iv|ct|mac')` with IV-len-16 + MAC-len-32 enforcement + `decrypt_encrypted_string(s, enc_key, mac_key)` constant-time HMAC verify via `subtle::ConstantTimeEq` THEN AES-256-CBC decrypt + `parse_and_decrypt(body, password)` end-to-end orchestration returning unencrypted vault-items JSON ready for `bitwarden::parse_export_str` consumption. New Cargo dep: `hkdf = "0.12"` (RustCrypto, pure-Rust). 22 new tests including envelope round-trip + 4 rejection paths + KDF distinct-info pin + KDF determinism + KDF salt/password divergence + format strict-validation + decrypt round-trip + wrong-mac-key + wrong-enc-key 8-perturbation defence + tampered-ciphertext rejection + parse_and_decrypt end-to-end with realistic vault-items JSON + envelope-error propagation. Feature flag NOT used — deps are tiny (~50 LOC hkdf, pure Rust); flag was overkill. **FULLY CLOSED — verified 2026-06-02 Session 34 (verify-first, zero new code; the `[~]` + "follow-up" note were stale).** The `BitwardenJsonImporter` integration shipped too: `discover_entries` (`credentials/bitwarden.rs`) auto-detects `encrypted:true` via `export_is_encrypted` → routes to `parse_and_decrypt` → reuses the plaintext parser on the recovered vault; `with_password` builder + clear no-password / wrong-password (`password rejected`/HMAC) / account-key-unsupported errors (never a silent empty vault). `cli/init.rs:3245-3277` does a BOUNDED peek (`file_is_encrypted_export`, ≤16 MiB) and interactively prompts (`dialoguer::Password`) for the export password ONLY when the export is encrypted, threading it through `build_wizard_importer_list`. 6 importer-level tests (incl. a real AES-256-CBC+HMAC-SHA256+PBKDF2 synthetic-export decrypt round-trip + wrong-password + account-key-path) on top of the 22 primitive tests. The full chain (decrypt primitive → importer auto-route → wizard prompt) is live + tested.

Returns the actual credential list instead of the C-03 "deferred" warning. — 3d (carved out from C-03). **Windows half shipped 2026-05-27 Session 28** (commit `22f9d47`, ~1d): new `credentials::chrome_windows` module (Windows-gated via `#[cfg(target_os = "windows")] pub mod chrome_windows`) ships the full v10/v11 envelope decrypt path. `parse_and_unwrap_aes_key(json_body)` reads the `Local State` JSON, base64-decodes `os_crypt.encrypted_key`, strips the `b"DPAPI"` sentinel, calls `CryptUnprotectData` to unwrap the 32-byte AES-256-GCM key (length-checked + typed). `decrypt_chrome_password(aes_key, blob)` dispatches on the 3-byte prefix: `v10`/`v11` → AES-256-GCM with the 12-byte nonce at `[3..15]` + ciphertext+tag at `[15..]` (`aes-gcm 0.10` from RustCrypto, single Err variant `AesGcmDecrypt` collapses wrong-key/tag-mismatch/corrupt for side-channel hygiene); anything else → bare DPAPI fallback for legacy pre-2018 installs. `dpapi_decrypt(blob)` wraps `CryptUnprotectData` with safe FFI (LocalFree-on-success buffer cleanup + `GetLastError` surfacing + documented SAFETY comments per raw-pointer slot). `read_chrome_logins(login_data_path)` opens Login Data read-only via the bundled `rusqlite` (so concurrent Chrome write-locks don't block) and SELECT-s `origin_url + username_value + password_value` per row. `discover_chrome_credentials_windows(login_data, local_state)` is the full orchestration: per-entry failures fold into warnings (whole import doesn't abort on one corrupt row); whole-flow failures (missing Local State / DPAPI denied / SQLite open failed) surface as `Result::Err`. `ChromeImporter::discover_entries` now dispatches per-OS at compile time: Windows → `discover_entries_windows` (tokio::spawn_blocking around the sync rusqlite + FFI path so the async executor isn't blocked), other OSes → `discover_entries_deferred` (the previous warning shape, scoped to Linux + macOS until those chunks ship). `name()` also dispatches: `"Chrome Login Data (DPAPI + AES-256-GCM)"` on Windows. New Cargo dep: `aes-gcm = "0.10"` pinned under `[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]` (Linux + macOS C-03b will use AES-128-CBC + reuse the cross-platform `aes 0.8` + `cbc 0.1` from C-04b). `ChromeWindowsError` taxonomy (10 variants — every structural + crypto failure surfaces a distinct variant for operator-facing wizard warnings; importer collapses to one shape before audit chain). 13 new tests: v10 envelope round-trip / v11 envelope round-trip / wrong-key rejection (single AesGcmDecrypt variant proven) / truncated blob rejection / tampered-ciphertext rejection (single bit flip MUST surface as AesGcmDecrypt — critical drift guard against silent garbage-byte acceptance) / empty-plaintext-31-byte-blob edge / Local State JSON parse: malformed-json + missing-os_crypt + invalid-base64 + missing-DPAPI-prefix-surfaces-len / DPAPI round-trip via CryptProtectData helper (live FFI, Windows-only via `#[cfg(windows)]`) / end-to-end Local State unwrap with synthetic DPAPI-wrapped key (Windows-only) / DPAPI corrupt-blob rejection (Windows-only) / prefix constants pin. **Linux half shipped 2026-05-27 Session 28** (commit `7d8da58`, ~1d): new `credentials::chrome_linux` module (Linux-gated via `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")] pub mod chrome_linux`) ships the full v10/v11 + Secret Service decrypt path. Constants pinned: `V10_PREFIX`/`V11_PREFIX`/`SALTYSALT = b"saltysalt"`/`CHROME_CBC_IV` = 16 ASCII spaces/`CHROME_FALLBACK_PASSWORD = b"peanuts"`/`PBKDF2_ITERATIONS = 1`/`AES_KEY_BYTES = 16`. `derive_chrome_aes_key(password)` runs PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1 iter=1 (KAT-pinned via openssl CLI: `fbe065ba49ad509cae5d80a73f8fdf36` for `peanuts`). `decrypt_chrome_password_linux(aes_key, blob)` strips the 3-byte v10/v11 prefix + runs AES-128-CBC with the hardcoded 16-space IV; single `AesCbcDecrypt` Err variant collapses every crypto failure (matches Windows half + C-04b side-channel hygiene policy). `get_chrome_password_from_keyring()` is the async Secret Service lookup against `application=chrome`/`chromium`/`brave` (handles Chromium-derived browsers); falls back to `b"peanuts"` on any DBus/unlock/lookup failure so headless CI runners still surface readable passwords. `get_chrome_password_strict()` is the variant that surfaces every failure as `Err` for diagnostic CLI paths. `discover_chrome_credentials_linux(login_data)` is the full async orchestration: keyring password (with fallback) → derive AES key → `spawn_blocking` SQLite read → per-row decrypt + UTF-8 decode → per-entry failures fold into warnings. New Cargo dep under `[target.'cfg(target_os = "linux")'.dependencies]`: `secret-service = { version = "4", features = ["rt-tokio-crypto-rust"] }` (pure-Rust, transitively pure-Rust via `zbus` + UNIX sockets — no `libdbus` C linker dep). 13 new tests including PBKDF2 KAT + iter-drift guard + v10/v11 round-trip + wrong-key 8-perturbation defence + unrecognized-prefix rejection + empty-after-prefix rejection + end-to-end with `peanuts`-derived key + canonical constants pin. **macOS half shipped 2026-05-27 Session 28** (commit `1af2444`, ~1d): new `credentials::chrome_macos` module (macOS-gated via `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")] pub mod chrome_macos`) ships the v10/v11 + Login Keychain decrypt path. Same constants as Linux except **`PBKDF2_ITERATIONS = 1003`** (Chrome's macOS-specific hardcoded value — Chromium legacy quirk). New: `CHROME_KEYCHAIN_SERVICES = ["Chrome Safe Storage", "Chromium Safe Storage", "Brave Safe Storage"]` + `CHROME_KEYCHAIN_ACCOUNT = "Chrome"`. `derive_chrome_aes_key(password)` runs PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1 iter=1003 (KAT-pinned via openssl CLI: `c4ec4a51b76b89adef0ec2f8c1fff09b` for `peanuts`). `decrypt_chrome_password_macos(aes_key, blob)` same AES-128-CBC + 16-space IV as Linux. `get_chrome_password_from_keychain()` uses `security_framework::passwords::get_generic_password` to walk the Chromium-family service names + return the first hit. `discover_chrome_credentials_macos(login_data)` is the async orchestration: `spawn_blocking` around the sync keychain lookup (so Apple's potential UI prompt doesn't block the async executor) → derive AES key → `spawn_blocking` SQLite read → per-row decrypt + UTF-8 decode → per-entry failures fold into warnings. New Cargo dep under `[target.'cfg(target_os = "macos")'.dependencies]`: `security-framework = "3"` (Apple's de-facto Rust binding for Keychain Services, RustCrypto-adjacent maintenance). 10 new tests including PBKDF2 iter=1003 KAT + **the critical iter=1003 ≠ iter=1 drift guard** (proves macOS won't accidentally land on Linux's iteration count if a future refactor tries to share the PBKDF2 wrapper) + v10/v11 round-trip + wrong-key 8-perturbation defence + unrecognized-prefix rejection + end-to-end with derived key + canonical constants pin including service-name family coverage. `ChromeImporter::discover_entries` now dispatches all three live OSes at compile time (`discover_entries_windows` / `discover_entries_linux` / `discover_entries_macos`); `discover_entries_deferred` falls back only on truly unsupported OSes. `ImportedCredential` import gated to `any(target_os = "windows", target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")`. The `importer_name_signals_implementation_state` drift-guard test now covers all three OSes.

Module-doc rewritten to correct the AES-GCM → AES-CBC algorithm fact + document the real ASN.1 SECITEM envelope shape (`SEQUENCE { key_id, AlgorithmIdentifier { OID 2.16.840.1.101.3.4.1.42, IV }, ciphertext }`). New Cargo deps: `aes 0.8`, `cbc 0.1` (with `block-padding`), `pbkdf2 0.12`, `subtle 2`. **Phase 2 chunk 1 shipped 2026-05-27 Session 27** (~3h): new `credentials::firefox_envelope` module with `FirefoxAlgorithm` enum (`#[non_exhaustive]`: `Aes256Cbc` / `TripleDesCbc` / `Unsupported(String)` — runtime dispatch by OID so both modern AES envelopes AND legacy 3DES envelopes round-trip through one parser) + `FirefoxEnvelope { key_id, algorithm, iv, ciphertext }` + `parse_firefox_envelope(b64)` strict-shape BER decoder + `EnvelopeError` taxonomy (`Base64` / `Asn1` / `OuterNotSequence` / `OuterArity` / `FieldNotOctetString` / `AlgIdNotSequence` / `AlgIdArity` / `AlgIdNotOid` / `AlgIdIvNotOctetString` / `IvLenMismatch`). IV-length enforced per algorithm (16 for AES-256-CBC, 8 for 3DES; Unsupported skips the check so the importer can surface the unknown OID cleanly without forcing a parser update). New Cargo dep: `simple_asn1 0.6`. 8 new tests with hand-rolled BER fixtures pinning the wire-format: canonical-AES-envelope-roundtrip / 3des-envelope-parses-as-TripleDesCbc-variant / wrong-IV-length-rejected (catches malicious 8-byte IV smuggled into AES path) / unknown-OID-accepts-without-IV-check / truncated-outer-sequence-rejected / outer-arity-mismatch-rejected / invalid-base64-rejected / outer-not-sequence-rejected (caller passed inner OCTET STRING by mistake) + drift-guard `expected_iv_len_matrix_pinned`. **Phase 2 chunk 2a shipped 2026-05-27 Session 27** (~30min): new `credentials::firefox_key4db` module with `derive_masking_key_pbkdf2_sha256(password, salt, iterations) -> [u8; 32]` PBKDF2-SHA256 wrapper using the `pbkdf2 0.12` crate added in Phase 1 + `verify_password_check(masking_key, iv, check_ciphertext) -> bool` which routes decrypt through Phase 1's `decrypt_aes256_cbc_pkcs7` and uses `subtle::ConstantTimeEq` to compare against the canonical `PASSWORD_CHECK_PLAINTEXT = b"password-check"`. Every failure mode (decrypt-fail / pad-fail / plaintext-mismatch) collapses to `false` so the side-channel surface stays minimal. 7 new tests: PBKDF2-SHA256 1-iter KAT + 2-iter KAT (cross-referenced against OpenSSL evp_kdf-pbkdf2 + Bouncy Castle test vectors) + empty-password-no-panic + iters-actually-wired drift guard + verifier-accepts-right-key + verifier-rejects-wrong-key (8-trial perturbation) + verifier-rejects-wrong-plaintext-at-right-key (critical drift guard against tampered key4.db blobs) + verifier-rejects-empty-ciphertext + canonical-constant-pin. **Phase 2 chunk 2b shipped 2026-05-27 Session 28** (commit `3f21c07`, ~4h): new `credentials::firefox_pbes2` module with strict BER decoder for the NSS PBES2 envelope shape (`SEQUENCE { AlgId(PBES2 { PBKDF2(salt, iters, keyLen, prf-hmacWithSHA256), AES-256-CBC(iv) }), ciphertext }`); pins every OID, rejects `hmacWithSHA1` PRF (downgrade attack guard), enforces NSS's 14-byte IV-inner quirk + helper `Pbes2Envelope::full_aes_cbc_iv()` reconstructs the 16-byte AES-CBC IV via the documented `[0x04, 0x0e] || iv_inner` prepend. Extended `credentials::firefox_key4db` with `Key4DbReader` trait + concrete `RusqliteKey4DbReader` (read-only open so concurrent Firefox write-locks don't block) + atomic reads `metaData WHERE id='password'` (item1=global_salt + item2=password-check envelope) + `nssPrivate.a11` (master-key envelope) + `derive_intermediate_key(global_salt, primary_password) -> [u8; 20]` SHA-1 KDF preamble (NSS quirk every firefox_decrypt derivative implements) + `extract_master_key<R: Key4DbReader>(reader, primary_password)` end-to-end orchestration (parses both envelopes → PBKDF2-SHA256 derive → AES-256-CBC unwrap on each → constant-time `ct_eq` gate against canonical `b"password-check"` → returns variable-length master-key blob, 24 bytes legacy 3DES or 32 bytes modern AES) + `extract_master_key_from_file(path, primary_password)` convenience wrapper opening the on-disk key4.db read-only via rusqlite. New Cargo dep: `sha1 = "0.10"` (RustCrypto pure-Rust, no new C deps; required for the NSS SHA-1 KDF preamble — NOT for password hashing). 15 new tests: PBES2 canonical-shape round-trip / IV-quirk reconstruction / truncated-outer / arity-one-field / wrong-outer-OID / IV-inner-wrong-length / wrong-PRF-OID (SHA-1 downgrade guard) / iters-1 / iters-100k / iters-above-signed-max + SHA-1 KAT for derive_intermediate_key + concatenation-order drift guard + extract_master_key end-to-end (32-byte AES key + 24-byte legacy 3DES key + empty-primary-password path + wrong-password rejection + too-short-blob rejection + envelope-parse error propagation + missing-row error propagation) + RusqliteKey4DbReader in-memory round-trip for both reads + end-to-end extract through the real rusqlite path + missing-metadata-row and missing-nssPrivate-row error paths. **Phase 2 chunk 3 shipped 2026-05-27 Session 28** (commit `b9cc1e1`, ~1h): refactored `credentials::firefox::FirefoxImporter` from unit struct to carry `primary_password: SecretString` with two constructors (`FirefoxImporter::new(SecretString)` for interactive wizard supplying operator-prompted password / `FirefoxImporter::with_no_primary_password()` non-interactive + vast-majority default). Real `discover_entries`: profile-root → profiles.ini → default-profile pick → logins.json parse → key4.db `extract_master_key_from_file` → per-entry SECITEM unwrap + AES-256-CBC decrypt → `ImportedCredential`. Per-entry failures fold into warnings (whole import doesn't abort on one corrupt row); whole-flow failures (missing profile / wrong password / corrupt key4.db) surface as `Result::Err` so the orchestrator's per-source diagnostic captures them. Legacy 3DES path: `extract_master_key` returns the full blob (24 bytes for legacy installs); FirefoxImporter rejects with an actionable `have Firefox migrate it first` error rather than silently truncating to 24 bytes + using a 3DES primitive we don't ship. Updated `credentials::wizard_step::default_importer_chain` for the new constructor surface. Tests: `importer_is_available_returns_bool_without_panic` / `importer_discover_surfaces_error_when_no_profile_exists` (CI runner has no Firefox profile → discover MUST surface `Err`; dev-machine with real profile early-returns to skip the negative case) / `importer_name_describes_aes_256_cbc_path` / `importer_constructors_compile` smoke test.
- [x] **C-05** Wizard step + settings panel parity + `services_redacted=true` WAL field per A5 SC-17. — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25 (wizard step half; settings-panel GUI half tracked as C-05b for the GUI lane). New `cli::init::step6g_credential_import` runs between `step6f_import_jarvis` and `step7_autonomy`. Interactive only — non-interactive (CI / scripted bring-up) skips because credential import requires explicit operator intent. Opt-in dialoguer Confirm + optional Bitwarden JSON export path prompt; declined operators see "Skipped — operator declined." Calls `credentials::wizard_step::build_wizard_importer_list(bitwarden_path)` (Bitwarden JSON if path supplied + Chrome + Firefox always) → `run_wizard_step(importers, "primary", ts_unix)`. Operator sees the summary list with per-source ok/fail + entry/warning counts + the SC-17 affirmation `services_redacted = true (entry_count = N)`. New helper `write_credential_import_sidecar(neoth_dir, ts_unix, &payload)` drops the SC-17-redacted payload to `~/.neoth/credentials_import_<ts>.json` via atomic `.tmp` + rename (Windows-safe). The daemon's next boot picks up the sidecar, emits the `0xD6 CREDENTIAL_IMPORT` WAL frame, then deletes the sidecar — at-least-once semantics mirror the cluster audit ingester. **The sidecar carries ONLY the redacted projection** — no secret material ever reaches disk via this path. SC-17 invariant test pins the disk shape: no `password`/`secret` substrings in the JSON, only `services_redacted` + `entry_count` + `services_hash` keys. 2 new tests pinned: redacted-payload-only / atomic-overwrite-no-tmp-leak. Settings-panel GUI parity (C-05b) lights up once the GUI lane consumes the same `wizard_step` module.

### v0.3 — Auto-update (operator wish-list B)

> **Current implementation correction (2026-07-15; supersedes the historical
> Session-25/MV-01b mechanics below):** self-update no longer calls
> `atomic_replace_binary`, exposes `backup_path`, or restores caller-managed
> `*.bak` files. Authenticated archives are closed-extracted and the complete
> compiled release profile is committed by one durable `InstallTransaction`,
> with the locally generated portable-ownership marker before the public core.
> A running Windows portable CLI uses a signed target-binary PID handoff and
> emits `0xD2` only after the child transaction commits. Registered Inno,
> native Linux DEB/RPM, and signed macOS App layouts fail closed; selecting and
> executing their exact signed platform installer/package is still open under
> `GOLD-R4-02` and must not be inferred as complete from the historical rows.

- [x] **U-01** Self-update for the `neoth` binary (signed-release verify + atomic swap + rollback). Reuses W-05a `PkgManagerHandle` trait. — 3d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25 (probe + cron-builder + daemon spawn + operator-action all shipped). Probe half added Session 25 (see U-04 entry). The atomic-swap half was already shipped earlier in `updater::self_update`: `apply_update(release, target, "neoth", install_dir)` runs the full download → sha256 verify → archive-extract → `atomic_replace_binary` (with `backup_path_for` rollback) chain. `cli::update::run_self_apply(repo, output)` wires it behind `neoth update --self --apply`. **WAL `0xD2 SELF_UPDATE_APPLIED` audit frame now emitted 2026-05-29 Session 28c** at the manual `--self --apply` path via new `emit_self_update_applied(outcome, repo, target)` — best-effort one-shot WAL writer (HF-01 pattern) guarded by `live_daemon_pid` so the CLI never opens a 2nd writer on a segment the daemon owns (single-writer safety: skip+log when daemon live). Payload `{from_version, to_version, backup_path, repo, target_triple, ts_unix}`. RESERVED entry removed from `tests/wal_emit_sites.rs`; SC-01a static check now validates the wiring. Daemon-auto-apply path (MV-01b Option A) is a separate item. `updater::probes::neoth_self_specs_async(gate)` composes the existing `self_update::check_for_update("The-Geek-Freaks/NEOTH")` (GitHub Releases API + user-agent + 403/404 error mapping shipped earlier) with `pipeline::neoth_self_specs` into a 1-component spec list. `neoth_self_specs_blocking` is the sync wrapper for the U-04 cron-builder closure (uses `Handle::try_current().block_on()` — safe because the closure runs on `spawn_blocking`; returns empty Vec outside a runtime instead of panicking). Wired into `cli::serve::run_serve` so `neoth serve` spawns the neoth_self updater cron on startup with the default 6h interval. 2 new tests: async-probe-yields-single-spec / blocking-outside-runtime-empty.
- [x] **U-02** Skills + plugins auto-update — `~/.neoth/skills/<id>/skill.yaml` + `plugins/<id>/plugin.json` re-resolve on schedule. Operator chooses cadence. — 3d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25 (scanner + cron-builder + daemon-spawn half; the actual upstream-registry resolver is U-02b). New `updater::probes::scan_installed_skills(home)` walks `~/.neoth/skills/<id>/skill.yaml`, parses via `serde_yaml::from_str::<SkillManifest>`, returns `Vec<(id, version)>`. Malformed YAMLs skip silently — the skill registry's own load path surfaces the parse error elsewhere; the probe stays observability-only. Plugins follow the same pattern via `scan_installed_plugins(home)` reading `~/.neoth/plugins/<id>/plugin.toml`. `skill_plugin_specs_for_home(home, gate)` composes both scanners into a single `skill_plugin_specs` Vec. **Every spec pairs current_version with `Err(NO_REGISTRY_RESOLVER_MSG)`** until U-02b ships the real registry resolver — honest audit signal, not a false "up_to_date" claim. `skill_plugin_specs_blocking(home, gate)` is the sync wrapper for the cron-builder closure; captures `home` by value so each tick re-reads disk (operator-added skills become visible without daemon restart). Wired into `cli::serve::run_serve` as the third updater lane (`UpdaterTaskKind::SkillPlugin`) alongside U-01 + U-03. 5 new tests: scan-missing-dir-empty / scan-lists-one-per-id / scan-skips-dirs-without-manifest / plugin-scan-empty / specs-for-home-pairs-with-no-registry-err.
- [x] **U-02b** Skill upstream registry resolver via per-skill `source` field. — 3d → ~2h — ✅ 2026-05-27 Session 26 (option 1 picked: per-skill `source: git+https://…` field + `git ls-remote --tags` probe + highest-semver pick). New optional `source: Option<String>` field on `SkillManifest`. New `updater::skill_resolver` module: `parse_git_source` accepts `git+{https,http,ssh}://…` and rejects everything else with a structured error; `parse_ls_remote_tags` dedupes annotated-tag `^{}` peels; `pick_highest_semver_tag` orders numerically (so `v10.0.0 > v2.0.0`, not lex-wise) and skips pre-release / non-triple tags; `resolve_latest_version(source)` shells out via `tokio::process::Command` with a 5s timeout + kill_on_drop + stderr capture. `updater::probes::scan_installed_skills_rows` returns the new richer `InstalledSkillRow { name, version, source }` shape; the legacy `scan_installed_skills` is a thin alias kept for back-compat callers. `skill_plugin_specs_for_home_async` is the new resolver-aware composer: skills with `source` land their real-or-Err latest_version; skills WITHOUT source keep the `NO_REGISTRY_RESOLVER_MSG` sentinel so the audit chain distinguishes "operator hasn't opted in" from "resolver failed". `skill_plugin_specs_blocking` swaps in the async path via `Handle::try_current().block_on()` (safe inside spawn_blocking). Plugins keep the sentinel — same `source` field addition to `PluginManifest` follows as a parallel commit. 11 tests: parse-git-source-accepts-https / accepts-ssh / rejects-missing-prefix / rejects-unknown-inner-scheme / parse-ls-remote-picks-tag-names / parse-ls-remote-empty-input / parse-semver-strips-v / parse-semver-rejects-prerelease / parse-semver-rejects-non-triple / pick-highest-orders-numerically / pick-highest-skips-non-semver / pick-highest-no-semver-returns-none / resolve-latest-bad-scheme-errs / resolve-latest-unreachable-host-errs + 3 probes-side: scan-rows-carries-source / async-specs-sentinel-for-skills-without-source / async-specs-attempts-resolver-for-skills-with-source. Closes the original "U-02b registry resolver" tier-5 item with the simpler-of-two-options design call documented inline (community registry `registry+https://…` lands when an operator-facing reason surfaces).

Probe half added Session 25 (see U-04 entry). Auto-bump half was already shipped earlier: `updater::apply_one(component)` runs `npm install -g <pkg>@latest` per CLI; `updater::check_and_apply_all()` orchestrates probe → conditional apply for every flagged CLI; `cli::update::run_clis_apply()` wires the orchestrator behind `neoth update --clis --apply`. Per-CLI npm installs use the Windows-aware `installers::build_cmd` indirection so the subprocess works identically on cmd.exe + bash. `updater::probes::cli_version_specs_async(gate)` reuses the existing `updater::check_all()` (subprocess `<binary> --version` + `npm view <pkg> version`; both shipped earlier) and projects every installed CLI into a `ComponentSpec` via `pipeline::cli_version_specs`. Absent CLIs produce no spec entry (matches the `Option`-skip contract). `npm view` failure surfaces as `latest_version: Err("npm view <pkg> version: failed")` so `compute_outcome` yields `ComponentStatus::Failed` — the WAL audit chain still proves the cron ran. `cli_version_specs_blocking` is the sync wrapper for the U-04 cron-builder. Wired into `cli::serve::run_serve` as the second updater lane alongside U-01. Both cron tasks tracked by serve.rs shutdown discipline (abort + await before WAL writer drains). 2 new tests: async-probe-only-installed-CLIs / blocking-outside-runtime-empty.
- [x] **U-04** Cron-task `0x4? updater task` writes WAL frame per check. Operator-readable `neoth updater status`. — 1d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25 (cron-loop + WAL emit + reader-side `neoth updater status` all shipped). Session 25 follow-up added `cli::updater::load_results_from_wal(segment)` — scans `~/.neoth/wal/000001.wal` (or `--wal-segment <path>` override) for `0x45 UPDATER_TASK_RESULT` frames, walks the frame chain via `decode_frame` + length math (`PREAMBLE_LEN + HEADER_BODY_LEN + reserved_len + payload_len + CRC_LEN`), deserializes each payload, filters non-0x45 frames silently. `--from-jsonl` still works for synthetic dry-runs; flags are mutually exclusive via clap's `conflicts_with`. Missing segment → empty Vec (operator hasn't started `neoth serve` yet) + the friendly "no record yet" render. 3 new tests on top of the cron tests: missing-segment-empty / too-short-segment-empty / live-WAL-roundtrip (spawns writer, emits one BOOT + one UPDATER_TASK_RESULT, asserts only the 0x45 round-trips). Operator runs `neoth updater status` and sees real cron outcomes without external tools. New `daemon::updater_cron` module mirrors EL-01's doctor-cron pattern. `UpdaterCronConfig { enabled, interval_secs }` with `DEFAULT_UPDATER_INTERVAL_SECS = 6h` + 60s floor clamp. `spawn_updater_cron_loop(config, task_kind, builder, writer)` returns `Option<JoinHandle<()>>` (None when disabled — no idle tokio task for opt-out operators). The boxed `Fn() -> Vec<ComponentSpec>` builder runs ON each tick so a transient network failure becomes a Failed outcome instead of crashing the daemon. **`tokio::task::spawn_blocking` + `std::panic::catch_unwind`** isolates builder panics — a misbehaving probe never aborts the loop. Per tick: emit `0x44 UPDATER_TASK_FIRED` BEFORE the pass (audit chain proves the cron started even when the daemon is killed mid-pass) → run_updater_pass → emit `0x45 UPDATER_TASK_RESULT` with the full payload. `run_updater_tick` is the pure-fn helper tests use to exercise the WAL emit + builder composition without spinning up the full loop. 5 tests: default-config-pinned / interval-clamp-60s / interval-uses-configured-above-floor / tick-emits-both-frames / spawn-None-when-disabled / spawn-Some-when-enabled (immediate abort so ticker doesn't fire). U-01 / U-02 / U-03 builders (neoth_self / skill_plugin / cli_version) are tracked separately — each needs operator-config-driven `latest_version` probe wiring (e.g. GitHub Releases for neothd; `claude --version` subprocess for the CLI lane). The spec-list builders themselves shipped Session 24 as `pipeline::neoth_self_specs` / `skill_plugin_specs` / `cli_version_specs`.

### v0.3 — Crash recovery + UX gaps (from A2 + A4)

- [x] **R-01** Turn-Journal write-ahead JSONL for `neoth chat` mid-stream durability. New WAL band `0x05 TURN_JOURNAL_OPENED` / `0x06 TURN_JOURNAL_CLOSED`. — 1d (hermes adopt) — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. Two new WAL event codes shipped in events.rs: `EVENT_TYPE_TURN_JOURNAL_OPENED = 0x05` + `EVENT_TYPE_TURN_JOURNAL_CLOSED = 0x06` (band 0x00..=0x0F memory+recall, slots 0x05/0x06 were free). New `recovery::turn_journal` module with `TurnJournal::open(neoth_dir, turn_id)` → creates `~/.neoth/journals/<turn_id>.jsonl`, accepts `append(&TurnEvent)` calls (one JSONL line per event — `Started` / `ProviderRequest` / `ProviderChunk` / `ProviderResponse` / `ProviderError` variants), `close()` deletes the file (clean turn completion), Drop-without-close leaves the file on disk for the next launch's recovery scan to find (= "crash mid-turn" signal). Sidecar-JSONL rather than main-WAL because mid-stream provider chunks would bloat the audit chain. `scan_for_journals(home) -> Vec<JournalReport>` walks the journals dir + reports turn_id / size / line_count for `neoth recover --list` to surface alongside the bak reports. `opened_payload` + `closed_payload` helpers pin the WAL frame shape (turn_id + journal_path + ts_unix) so the chat dispatch + tests agree. Defensive: turn_id rejected for unsafe filename components (`../`, `/`, `\`, empty), append rejects raw newlines (serde_json already escapes them, but the guard pins the line-at-a-time scan contract for any future variant that bypassed serde). 12 unit tests across journal lifecycle (open / append / close / close-idempotent / drop-leaves-file / scan-missing-dir-empty / scan-orphan-surfaced / scan-skips-non-jsonl / opened-payload-canonical / closed-payload-line-count / open-rejects-unsafe-turn-id / serde-escapes-newlines-each-event-one-line). `cli::recover --list` now also surfaces orphan journals so the recovery-UX bundle (R-01 + R-02 + R-06) presents both bak files and crashed chat turns in one operator-facing view.
- [x] **R-02** `.bak` session recovery — snapshot-on-shrink + startup compare-and-restore. — 1d (hermes adopt) — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::recovery` module with two public surfaces: (1) `shrink_safe_write(path, new_content) -> Result<bool>` — atomic write via `.tmp` + rename; when the new content is strictly SHORTER than the existing file, first copy the existing file to `<path>.bak` so the pre-shrink state survives. Same-size or larger writes skip the bak step (preset-switch routine writes don't churn the disk). Returns the bak-was-written boolean for the audit trail. (2) `scan_for_baks(home) -> Vec<BakReport>` walks `home` + one level of subdirectories for `*.bak` files, pairs each with its live counterpart, classifies each via `BakVerdict::{Stale, LiveMissing, LiveShrunk, LiveOk}` for the R-06 UI layer to consume. Defensive: tolerates missing home, skips torn-write artifacts (`.tmp.bak` / `.bak.tmp`), one bak per tracked file (repeated shrinks overwrite — most-recent pre-shrink state, not unbounded archive; rolling backups belong to R-07). 11 unit tests: shrink-creates-bak / same-size-skips / grow-skips / fresh-file-skips / bak-path-canonical / scan-empty-on-missing-home / scan-empty-on-no-baks / scan-classifies-LiveOk-LiveShrunk-LiveMissing / scan-descends-one-subdir-for-wal-baks / scan-skips-torn-write-artifacts / second-shrink-overwrites-most-recent. **R-06 unblocked** (its UI surface consumes `scan_for_baks` directly).
- [x] **R-03** TPS metering SSE in chat header (1Hz streaming / 10Hz idle / 60min rolling). — 1d (hermes adopt) — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. Two halves: (1) new `Snapshot::chat_header_line()` formatter on `providers::meter` — renders `[meter] {out_tps:.1} tps out · p50 {p50:.0}ms · {n} samples` from a snapshot, returns `None` for cold meters (zero samples) so the first chat turn after boot suppresses the line instead of printing a noise `[meter] 0.0 tps … 0 samples`. (2) new `/metrics/tps` route on the daemon's existing HTTP listener (alongside `/healthz` + `/metrics`) — returns single-shot JSON `{output_tps, input_tps, p50_ms, p95_ms, sample_count, header_line}` so GUI / SSE clients poll on whatever cadence they need (the 1Hz-streaming / 10Hz-idle cadence is a client-side concern; daemon side stays a simple GET). 60min rolling window is the existing `DEFAULT_WINDOW` on the Meter — already shipped, no change needed. Returns `{}` (empty object) when no meter is wired so a GUI consumer renders an inert status bar instead of erroring out. 6 new tests across 2 modules: 3 meter tests (cold meter returns None / single-sample renders correctly / decimal-width stable drift guard) + 3 healthz tests (no-meter returns empty JSON / meter-wired returns all 6 fields including header_line with `[meter]` prefix / unknown sibling path /metrics/nope still 404). Full SSE streaming endpoint (long-lived connection with chunked transfer) deferred as v0.5 polish — the simple poll endpoint covers the operator-visible contract today.
- [x] **R-04** Wizard resume after crash — `wizard_checkpoint.json` after every step. **A4 zero-coverage gap.** — 0.5d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `cli::wizard_checkpoint` module persists `WizardState` (minus secrets — `provider_key` / `telegram_token` / `keet_seed_phrase` / `pears_bearer_token` are explicitly stripped) to `~/.neoth/wizard_checkpoint.json` via atomic `.tmp` + rename + mode-0600. `run_init` now calls `maybe_resume_from_checkpoint` before step1: on TTY → dialoguer Confirm "Resume from incomplete wizard (last updated 2 hours ago)? secrets must be re-entered"; on CI/pipe → silent auto-resume. Operator decline → file cleared. After every step body returns Ok → `save_checkpoint_best_effort` overwrites (failures log warn, never abort wizard). Final `.initialized` marker write → clear checkpoint (wizard finished). Corrupt JSON → log + delete + fresh wizard (defensive). 9 wizard_checkpoint tests (round-trip / secret-strip / atomic-rename / corrupt JSON / idempotent clear / zero-length file) + 5 init R-04 tests (no-checkpoint noop / non-interactive auto-resume / corrupt-file recovery / age-format unit boundaries + zero/negative) = 14/14 green. Privacy contract test pins that `PROVIDER-SECRET-DEADBEEF` and `TELEGRAM-TOKEN-BADBADBAD` literals never appear in the on-disk JSON even when set on the source state.

Want a quick tour?" **A4 delight moment.** — 0.5d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `step9_offer_start_daemon` runs after `.initialized` marker write (interactive only — CI/pipe skip so wizard stays scriptable). On Yes → `spawn_daemon_detached` invokes `std::env::current_exe()` with `serve` subcommand; Unix detaches via `Stdio::null()` on all three fds; Windows uses `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | DETACHED_PROCESS` so closing the wizard terminal doesn't SIGTERM the daemon. PID surfaced in the success line. `FIRST_TOUR_MARKER` ("first_tour_pending") + `FIRST_TOUR_MESSAGE` ("Hi, I'm running. Want a quick tour? Type `neoth chat \"give me a tour\"` or `neoth recall --help`...") constants — marker written either way (start now OR start later by hand both reach the onboarding-aware first chat). `consume_first_tour_marker` in `cli/chat.rs::run_chat_with` reads + deletes on the very first invocation post-wizard and prepends "[neoth] Hi, I'm running..." to the chat output; subsequent calls return None so the greeting never repeats. 5 new tests in `cli::init::tests`: write-creates-canonical / consume-returns-some-then-none / consume-returns-none-when-absent / write-idempotent-on-rewrite (overwrites stale content) / message-is-actionable-one-liner (drift-guards the operator-facing copy). The detached-spawn path itself is intentionally not unit-tested because forking a daemon in CI is fragile — the spawn helper is small + cross-platform-bracketed; integration test belongs in e2e-runner scope.
- [x] **R-06** `neoth recover` command — checks for `*.bak` + journal entries, presents "incomplete session from [ts] — resume?" UI layer for R-01/R-02. — 1d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `cli::recover` module with three modes on `RecoverArgs` (mutually exclusive): (1) `--list` (default) — print every `.bak` with live_path / live_size / bak_size / verdict (`LIVE_MISSING` / `LIVE_SHRUNK` / `LIVE_OK` / `STALE`) plus an action footer telling the operator how to restore-or-clean. Table + JSON output. (2) `--restore <LIVE_PATH>` — copy the `.bak` content over the live file via `recovery::shrink_safe_write`, which automatically snapshots the CURRENT live first (so an operator who restores the wrong file can re-restore from the new pre-restore bak). Operator passes the LIVE path (not the bak path) for UX symmetry with the bak naming convention. (3) `--clean [--yes]` — bulk-delete every bak classified `LiveOk` or `Stale` (safe-to-discard; live is same-or-larger). Confirms before destructive op per AGENTER hard rule; `--yes` skips the prompt for CI/non-interactive. **Critically**: `LiveShrunk` (possible data loss) and `LiveMissing` (operator should restore) baks are NEVER auto-deleted even with `--yes` — safety contract enforced at the verdict-filter layer. 6 unit tests pin every path: list-empty-no-error / restore-errors-without-bak / restore-writes-bak-to-live / clean-without-yes-no-delete / clean-with-yes-deletes-LiveOk-only / clean-leaves-data-loss-baks / clean-leaves-LiveMissing-baks. Recovery-UX bundle (R-02 + R-06) closed; the journal-entries half of R-06 spec belongs to R-01 (Turn-Journal write-ahead JSONL — still pending) and lights up automatically once R-01 ships.

### v0.3 — Accelerated from v0.4 (per A1 + A4)

- [x] **EL-01** `neoth doctor` cron + actionable findings + WAL audit. **A1 promote: day-1 trust builder for non-dev.** — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25. The pure-fn primitives shipped Session 24; Session 25 closed the runtime wiring Alex flagged ("EL-01 hat gute Datenformen, aber der Cron loop, WAL audit frame und Notification-Pfad müssen noch rein"). New `EVENT_TYPE_DOCTOR_TICK = 0x46` (free slot in the 0x4\* cron band, alongside 0x40-0x45) + a band-membership compile-asserts entry. New `daemon::doctor_cron::spawn_doctor_cron_loop(config, home, writer, sink) -> Option<JoinHandle>` returning None when `config.enabled == false` (operator opt-out, no idle tokio task) or Some(handle) wrapping a `tokio::interval(config.interval_duration())` loop. The handle is tracked alongside the existing cron scheduler in `cli::serve.rs` so daemon shutdown aborts + awaits it before draining the WAL writer (drain-before-writer-close discipline). Each tick calls the new pure-fn `run_doctor_tick(home, &writer, &sink)`: runs `cli::doctor::run_all_checks` → builds the existing `DoctorCronReport` → serialises to JSON → emits a `0x46 DOCTOR_TICK` WAL frame with `EventFlags::SYNTHETIC` (no operator turn produced it). The frame fires EVERY tick — clean or not — so the audit chain proves the cron actually ran, not just "the cron ran AND found things". Non-clean reports additionally hand `render_chat_notification(&report)` to a pluggable `DoctorNotificationSink` trait — the daemon wires `SidecarNotificationSink::new(home/notifications)` which writes `doctor_<ts>.json` files atomically (`.tmp` + Windows-safe rename), polled by the GUI notifications panel + future channel-push subscribers. `TracingNotificationSink` is the bare-bones fallback. 6 new tests pin every path: sidecar-writes-file-with-body (+ no .tmp leak after atomic rename) / tracing-sink-returns-ok / run_doctor_tick-writes-WAL-frame-and-returns-report / run_doctor_tick-doesnt-notify-on-clean / spawn_loop-returns-None-when-disabled / spawn_loop-returns-Some-when-enabled (with immediate abort so the ticker never fires in CI).
- [x] **OP-02** Hindsight session compression on session-end (oh-my-pi port). **A1 promote: cheap, high-impact on every session.** — 1.5d (MIT license confirmed) — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 25. The pure-fn compressor + atomic save/load primitives shipped earlier; Session 25 closed the runtime wiring Alex flagged ("muss bei /quit, Ctrl+C, Session close und Next-Session seed prompt automatisch laufen"). Three new helpers in `memory::hindsight`: (1) `session_id_for(ts_unix, prompt)` returns deterministic `chat-<ts>-<xxh3:016x>` ids — same prompt at the same second produces the same id, so re-runs in tests + the same-second seed-banner guard share the round-trip. (2) `save_session_card(home, ts_unix, prompt, reply)` builds a 2-turn transcript (operator + agent), runs `compress_session`, persists via the existing atomic save_card path. (3) `save_session_card_best_effort(...)` swallows IO errors → warn-log so the chat exit never fails on a hindsight write. (4) `latest_card(home)` returns the most-recent HindsightCard for next-session seeding. (5) `next_session_seed_banner(home, current_session_id)` renders `"[neoth] last session: <one_line_summary>"` with built-in guards: no prior card → empty, current_session_id matches latest → empty (suppresses same-second self-loop). Wired into `cli/chat.rs::run_chat_with`: startup (after first-tour greeting + after `resolve_prompt`) prints the seed banner; happy-path exit (just before `drop(writer)`) calls `save_session_card_best_effort` so every successful chat invocation produces a card. Error-path early-returns (provider failure, council quorum drop, MCP dispatch failure, etc.) intentionally skip the save: half a session isn't worth compressing. 8 new tests pin the helpers: session_id-deterministic + differs-on-ts + differs-on-prompt + length-pinned / save_session_card-writes-2-turn-card / best-effort-does-not-panic-on-invalid-path / latest_card-returns-most-recent / latest_card-none-for-empty / banner-renders-one-line-summary / banner-empty-without-prior-card / banner-empty-when-current-id-matches-latest. Future Ctrl+C interception is handled by the existing process-wide shutdown handler (R-04's `_pid_guard`); a SIGINT mid-turn drops the WAL writer cleanly + skips the hindsight save (correct — partial turn).
- [x] **P-01** 7-tier approval classifier (`readonly_scoped` / `readonly_search` / `mutating` / `exec_capable` / `control_plane` / `interactive` / `unknown`) — auto-approve safe tiers at `standard` autonomy. **A1+A4 critical: promoted to v0.3 — behavioral change, kills confirm fatigue before v0.5 surfaces multiply tool calls.** — 1.5d (openclaw adopt) — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 24. New `permissions::tier_classifier` module. `ApprovalTier { ReadonlyScoped, ReadonlySearch, Mutating, ExecCapable, ControlPlane, Interactive, Unknown }` snake_case + `as_str()` audit pin. `AutonomyForTier { Strict, Standard, Elevated, Full }` mirrors existing `super::AutonomyLevel` (Custom maps to Strict for safety until per-tier override lands). `TierDecision { AutoApprove, Prompt }`. **Decision matrix** (Tier × Autonomy): ControlPlane + Interactive + Unknown ALWAYS prompt (externally-visible side effects + classifier uncertainty); Strict prompts every tier; Standard auto-approves readonly only; Elevated adds Mutating; Full adds ExecCapable. `classify_tool(name)` verb-driven: `read/list/recall/status/show/find/describe` → ReadonlyScoped; `search/grep/scan/fetch` → ReadonlySearch (but `search.memory|kanban|audit` stays scoped); `write/edit/create/save/mark/delete/import/store` → Mutating; `exec/shell/run/spawn/bash` → ExecCapable; `config/enable/disable/schedule/revoke/grant/set_autonomy` + ALL `permissions.*` namespace → ControlPlane; `send/notify/invite/post/reply` → Interactive (checked FIRST so `send.email` doesn't false-match elsewhere). `classify_and_decide(tool, autonomy)` combined entry the dispatcher calls per invocation. 25 tests: 3× enum as_str + snake_case serde pins / ControlPlane always-prompts at all 4 autonomies / Interactive always-prompts / Unknown always-prompts / Strict prompts every tier / Standard auto-approves readonly only / Elevated adds Mutating / Full adds ExecCapable / classify empty=Unknown / unrecognised-verb=Unknown / 7 readonly_scoped verbs / 4 readonly_search verbs / search.memory|kanban|audit stays scoped / 8 mutating verbs / 5 exec verbs / 7 control_plane verbs / permissions.show stays ControlPlane (readonly verb under control namespace) / 5 interactive verbs / no-separator uses full string / classify_and_decide returns pair (read.file=ReadonlyScoped+AutoApprove, send.telegram=Interactive+Prompt-even-at-Full) / Unknown prompts at every autonomy. 25/25 tier_classifier suite green.

New `HooksAction::Trace { since, limit, segment }` subcommand: parses `since` via in-module `parse_since_to_ns` (accepts `30s` / `5m` / `2h` / `1d` — 12-line custom parser beats a humantime crate dep), computes `floor_ns = wall_now - window`, walks the default WAL segment (`~/.neoth/wal/000001.wal`, override via `--segment`), keeps frames whose `event_type ∈ 0x80..=0x83` AND whose `hlc.physical_ns >= floor_ns`. Renders table or JSON via existing `--output`. Negative priorities supported for kill-switch hooks. New `HookChainConfig { fail_fast: bool }` keyed by stage name in `FreedomConfig::hook_chain: HashMap<String, HookChainConfig>` (empty default = legacy lenient everywhere). `FreedomConfig::fail_fast_for_stage(stage)` helper looks up the policy. New `dispatcher::run_stage_with_config(stage, body, hooks, invoker, fail_fast)`: when `fail_fast=true`, a regex compile failure at this stage escalates from skip-and-warn to `StageOutcome::Block { reason: "fail_fast: regex compile failed ..." }` so a typo'd safety-hook matcher can't silently fall back to allow. `run_stage` + `run_stage_with_plugins` keep their pre-AR-03 signature as thin wrappers (back-compat). 2 loader tests (priority override / negative priority first) + 3 dispatcher tests (back-compat false / strict true escalates / strict doesn't affect well-formed hooks) + 2 config tests (absent section = lenient default / yaml round-trip with mixed stages). 27/27 across loader + dispatcher + config suites green. 5 FreedomConfig test-literals in `cli/chat.rs` updated with `hook_chain: Default::default(),`.

Renders table + JSON. - [x] **M-01** Hebbian reinforce on warm + cold tier hit. Currently `memory/tiers.rs:139::hebbian_reinforce_event` hardcoded to `idx_episode`. SPEC `PLAN/SPEC_memory_tiers.md` requires all-tier. — A5 M-1 — 1d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New tier-aware `hebbian_reinforce_at_tier(conn, tier, event_id, now_ns)` dispatches to `idx_episode` / `idx_consolidated` / `idx_longterm` per `Tier::{Hot,Warm,Cold}`. Hot wraps it for back-compat; warm uses `COALESCE(event_id, -id) = ?1` so both retained events + synthesised per-day summary rows match the negative `-id` ids that recall surfaces; cold uses plain `event_id = ?1`. `cli/recall.rs` pre-fix had `if h.tier != "hot" { continue; }` which silently skipped warm + cold hits — frequently-recalled warm rows would decay at 0.99/day without counter-push, eventually falling below `PROMOTION_THRESHOLD` and dropping at the 90-day boundary instead of promoting to cold. Replaced with tier-string match → `Tier` enum (groundtruth/unknown labels skip per `GT-3` decay-immune rule) → `hebbian_reinforce_at_tier` call. 5 new tier-aware tests in `memory/tiers.rs`; 20/20 tier suite + 188/188 memory suite + 9/9 recall suite green.

Best-effort — failures log warn but never abort recall reply. Operators reading `neoth wal show --type importance_reinforced` now see CLI-recall + daemon-path reinforcements with identical frame shape.

New `ConsentDecision { AllowOnce, AllowAlways, Deny }` enum with `serde(rename_all = "snake_case")` for stable wire shape + `as_str()` for audit + `allows()` (only Deny is false) + `persists()` (only AllowAlways flips the marker bit). `parse_decision(s)` accepts canonical strings (`allow_once`/`allow_always`/`deny`) AND numeric menu picks (`1`/`2`/`3`) AND common aliases (`yes`/`always`/`no`) — case-insensitive, whitespace-trimmed. `apply_decision(home, kind, decision)` writes the existing `.granted` marker only for AllowAlways, leaves marker untouched for AllowOnce + Deny (Deny does NOT auto-revoke prior AllowAlways — pinned by test; operator uses explicit `neoth consent revoke` for that). Non-cloud kinds short-circuit to no-op so callers can pipe every kind through. `consent_decision_payload(kind, decision, source, ts_unix) -> Vec<u8>` builds the canonical `{kind, decision, source, ts_unix}` JSON for the new WAL event `EVENT_TYPE_CONSENT_DECISION = 0x65` (chose 0x65 not 0x30 because the 0x30..=0x3F band is already occupied by Channels; 0x65 is adjacent to the council/decisions band 0x60..=0x6F which is the semantic neighborhood for operator decisions — band choice documented inline). 10 new tests: allows + persists pin / as_str pin / parse_decision canonical + numeric + aliases (case-insensitive + whitespace-trim) / parse_decision garbage returns None / apply AllowAlways writes marker + idempotent / apply AllowOnce no marker / apply Deny no marker + does NOT auto-revoke prior grant (safety contract) / non-cloud is no-op for every variant / payload carries required fields / serde round-trip drift guard. Existing two-state `ensure_granted_or_prompt` left untouched for back-compat; tri-state surface is the foundation for the upcoming `cli::consent decide <kind>` operator command + the eventual chat-side prompt rewrite.

New `crate::domain_events` module. `DomainEvent` enum is `#[non_exhaustive]` (adding a variant is back-compat for both producers + consumers because callers match with `* => {}`) + `serde(tag = "kind", rename_all = "snake_case")`for stable on-wire shape. Five initial variants:`CouncilWinnerSelected`/`SubAgentDispatched`/`CronJobFired`/`ProviderResponded`/`WalFrameAppended`— each carries the smallest copy-able payload (ids + timestamps + counts; consumers that want full rows read back from canonical store, NOT from the bus).`EventBus::with_capacity(n)`clamps to ≥1 (defence against tokio broadcast's panic-on-zero),`publish(event) -> usize`returns recipient count (0 is NOT an error — quiet bus is fine),`subscribe()`returns`broadcast::Receiver`whose cursor starts at current position (no history replay for late joiners). Consumers MUST handle`RecvError::Lagged(n)`("missed N events, keep going" contract documented inline). Bus is cheaply clonable via`Arc<broadcast::Sender>`inside — every clone shares the same channel. Not a WAL replacement: events are in-memory + ephemeral + fire-and-forget; producers that want both fire WAL FIRST (so audit survives a crash) and bus SECOND. 11 tests: kind() drift guard for all 5 variants / serde round-trip preserves snake_case`"kind":` tag / publish into empty bus returns 0 / single-subscriber receives / multi-subscriber each gets copy / late joiner doesn't replay / slow consumer gets Lagged then recovers / clone shares channel / receiver_count tracks live subscribers / capacity ≥1 even when caller passes 0 / Debug fmt surfaces receiver_count for live diagnostics. Council + sub_agents + cron + Self-correction loop can now subscribe without producers knowing who's listening; adding the 6th variant in v0.9 is a single-file change.
- [x] **P-04** Tiered RPC dispatch + `redact_params_for_log` — secret-key redaction before any debug trace. — A2 (openhuman adopt) — 1d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `security::redact::redact_params_for_log(&serde_json::Value) -> serde_json::Value` recursively walks JSON, redacts every string leaf through the existing `redact_text` pattern band. Plus new `is_sensitive_field_name(&str) -> bool` field-name guard that redacts ANY value (regardless of length / pattern match) when the JSON key matches a canonical set: `api_key` / `apikey` / `token` / `access_token` / `refresh_token` / `bearer` / `password` / `passwd` / `pwd` / `secret` / `authorization` / `auth` / `cookie` / `x-api-key` / `x_api_key` / `client_secret` / `private_key` / `session_id` / `session` (all case-insensitive). Field-name guard catches the case where a short secret (e.g. 4-char API key in test fixtures) wouldn't trip the regex band but the field NAME tells us it's sensitive. Design choice (drift guard pinned): walk-leaves over stringify-then-redact — stringifying first would escape every quote, leaving `"sk-..."` instead of `sk-...`, and the `\b` word boundary in the regex band wouldn't fire. Walking leaves preserves the original JSON shape + sanitises each string value at its actual content boundary. 7 new tests + existing 14 regex-pattern tests = 21/21 redact suite green. Helper is ready for adoption by every future `tracing::debug!(params = ?body)` call site in `n8n_api`, `cli/serve`, and the JSON-RPC dispatch layer — no callsite churn in this commit, just the safe-by-default primitive available.

New `crate::event_ledger` module. Lightweight JSON sidecar at `~/.neoth/event_ledger.json` updated incrementally per WAL append; GUI cold-start reads + parses in <5 ms with `serde_json` instead of the 50-200 ms WAL decode. `Ledger { counts_by_event_type: HashMap<String, u64>, recent: Vec<RecentEvent>, recent_capacity, last_updated_unix }`. `RecentEvent { event_id, event_type, ts_ns }` — only the metadata the GUI summary needs, payload bodies stay in the WAL. `record(event, now_unix)` bumps the per-event-type counter (key format `"0x01"` uppercase hex with `0x` prefix — operator `jq` query convention) + pushes onto the bounded ring (trims oldest at capacity) + stamps `last_updated_unix`. Defensive: `with_capacity(0)` clamps to 1; `record` self-heals from `recent_capacity=0` to `DEFAULT_RECENT_CAPACITY=256`. `top_event_types(n)` sorts desc by count then alphabetical for stable output. `save(path, &ledger)` atomic `.tmp` + rename via existing `credentials::write_mode_0600` (inherits WAL trust boundary). `load(path)` returns `Ok(Ledger::new())` for missing file OR zero-length (atomic-rename race window) so daemon bootstrap + GUI cold-start both call unconditionally. 12 tests: new-ledger-empty / with_capacity-zero-clamps-to-one / record-increments + pushes-recent / record-trims-oldest-at-capacity (5 events into cap-3 → only last 3 survive; counters cumulative regardless) / record-recovers-from-zero-capacity (self-healing) / event-type-keys-format `0x01` uppercase pin / top_event_types desc + alphabetical tie-break / save+load round-trip preserves shape / load-default-on-missing / load-default-on-zero-length (race-window defensive) / load-propagates-parse-error-on-garbage / save-atomic-via-tmp-rename. Single-writer contract: daemon's shared state owns a `Mutex<Ledger>`; GUI is read-only. Reactive streaming (live updates while GUI is open) is the follow-up — the cold-start surface is what was blocking GUI bootstrap.

New `crate::proactive` module. `ProactiveItem { priority: i32, dedup_key, channel, source, body, scheduled_for_unix }`. `ProactiveQueue::enqueue(item)` returns `false` on duplicate dedup_key (prior item wins per spec); `drain(now_unix, cap)` pops priority-desc with `scheduled_for_unix <= now_unix` tie-broken by earliest-scheduled-first, capped by both `cap` arg AND remaining daily budget (`config.max_per_day = 3` default per AGENTER hard rule, widenable via `with_config`). Daily-cap window is a 24h rolling list of drain timestamps so a second drain inside the same window respects the budget left from the first; budget resets after 24h rolls past.
