### v0.4 — n8n smarts + MCP token wins (A1 keeps n8n adopt)

- [x] **N-04** Smart MCP loader — don't ship all MCPs in every chat context; load on first slash-command / first explicit reference. — 2.5d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::mcp::smart_loader` module. Pure-fn classifier decides per-turn which MCP servers ship their full tool catalogue into the system prompt + which stay deferred behind a one-line "available on demand" hint. Two trigger signals (in priority order): (1) **Explicit reference** — `/<server-name>` slash command OR server name as a tokenized word in the prompt (word-boundary, NOT substring — pre-rule pinned by test that `"raw.githubusercontent.com"` does NOT trigger the `github` server); (2) **Tool-name match** — case-insensitive substring of any of the server's tool names. Servers matching either are active; rest are deferred. Empty tool-name strings explicitly do NOT match every prompt (defensive guard pinned by test). `LoadDecision { server_name, active, reason: LoadReason }`. `LoadReason { ExplicitReference, ToolNameMatch, NoTrigger }` (snake_case wire form). `LoadPlan` carries `decisions: Vec<LoadDecision>` + `active_servers()` / `deferred_servers()` / `deferred_count()` helpers. `render_deferred_hint(plan, servers) -> Option<String>` produces the terse single-line hint with correct singular/plural ("1 tool" vs "0/2 tools") + returns None when every server is active. Token savings: with 4-5 MCP servers × 10+ tools each loading ~3-8 KiB of catalogue per turn, the loader-driven scope drops most turns to 0-1 active servers + a ~30-byte deferred hint. 15 tests: reason_as_str pin / slash-prefix activates / word-boundary mention activates / substring-inside-larger-word does NOT trigger explicit (drift guard) / tool-name substring activates / case-insensitive tool match / no-trigger defers / empty-tool-name does NOT match all prompts / explicit-reference wins over tool-match (priority order pin) / multi-server partition active+deferred / empty server list returns empty plan / deferred hint lists tool counts / hint pluralises 1=tool 2=tools (with long unique tool names to avoid substring false-fires) / hint returns None when all active / hint returns None for empty server list. CLI wiring into chat dispatch (call `plan_loader` before assembling MCP catalogue + render via existing `mcp::catalogue::assemble_catalogue`) is a 20-line cli/chat extension when wired.

New `crate::claude_plugins` module — distinct from `crate::wasm_plugin` (which owns the NEOTH-native WASM `plugin.toml` sandbox manifest). `ClaudePluginManifest` mirrors Anthropic's Claude-Code plugins ecosystem `plugin.json` shape: `name` / `version` / `description` / `display_name` / `author` (untagged enum — accepts bare-string OR `{name,email,url}` object) / `commands` (slash command md paths) / `agents` (subagent system-prompt md paths) / `skills` (Skill md paths) / `mcp_servers` (path to `.mcp.json`) / `hooks` (path to hooks config) / `homepage` / `license` (SPDX). `parse_manifest(&[u8])` deserialises + calls `validate_manifest`: name must be non-empty kebab-or-snake-case starting with a letter (≤ 64 chars), version semver-shaped (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH[-pre][+build]`), every relative path rejected if absolute (unix `/...` AND Windows `C:\...`) OR contains a `..` segment anywhere (path-traversal defence). 13 tests cover: minimal manifest / full manifest with every optional field / bare-string author / 6 invalid names rejected / 5 valid names accepted / 7 invalid versions rejected / 7 valid versions accepted (incl. `1.0.0-rc.1+sha.abc`) / absolute path rejected / Windows absolute path rejected / parent-traversal anywhere in path rejected / parse error surfaces with context / name > 64 chars rejected. Discovery + registration of the parsed assets is a follow-up; the parser primitive unblocks every future call site by giving them a typed shape.
- [x] **Q-02** Book of Secret Knowledge → TOML skill catalog parser. — 0.5d (MIT) — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::bosk` module with `SkillCatalog` + `SkillEntry` + `CatalogMeta` types mirroring a TOML distillation of github.com/trimstray/the-book-of-secret-knowledge. Each `[[entry]]` carries `name` / `category` (slash-delimited like `cli/search`) / `description` (required non-empty) / optional `url` (must be `http://` or `https://`) / optional `install` command / `tags` (lowercased on parse for case-insensitive recall). Catalog-level `[meta]` block keeps source URL + upstream revision for attribution when operators fork the upstream catalog. `parse_catalog(&[u8]) -> Result<SkillCatalog, CatalogError>` rejects: empty name / empty description / empty category / duplicate name within the catalog (with `first_idx` pointer for the operator's deduplication) / non-http URL scheme (defence against `javascript:` / `ftp://` / local paths smuggled into the catalog). Helpers on `SkillCatalog`: `find(name)` for exact lookup, `by_category_prefix(prefix)` for `cli` → matches `cli/search` + `cli/files`, `all_tags()` returns sorted distinct set for the catalog summary. 13 tests cover: empty catalog / minimal entry / full entry with every optional field + meta block (incl. tag lowercasing drift guard) / 4 reject paths (empty name / whitespace-only description / empty category / duplicate name) / non-http URL rejection × 4 (javascript: / ftp:// / absolute path / empty) / http + https accepted / find exact-match-only (case-sensitive on name per design) / by_category_prefix matches exact AND subpath / all_tags returns sorted distinct lowercased / parse-error surfaces with context. Indexing the catalog into recall + a `neoth bosk lookup` CLI subcommand are follow-ups; the parser primitive unblocks both.
- [x] **WA-SEAM-01** WhatsApp Graph-API base-URL test seam + wiremock send-arm coverage. **From Session 39 channel-gov gremium (poc_mock_seam) — built now, not deferred, per Alex's no-defer rule (~1d in-scope work).** — ~1d — ✅ 2026-06-05 Session 39. Added `whatsapp_api::GRAPH_API_BASE` const + base-URL-injectable `send_text_message_at` / `validate_token_at` cores; the public `send_text_message` / `validate_token` are thin wrappers passing the production base (back-compat for `whatsapp.rs` + `cli/channel.rs` callers). `WhatsAppSendCreds` gained `base_url: Option<String>` (`None` = production); the webhook send arm uses it. The two P0 tests now point at a `wiremock::MockServer` and MACHINE-assert the "skips API" half — `received_requests()` empty on Deny + DryRun — and a NEW `p0_send_hits_api_once_and_audits_delivered_egress` proves Allow → exactly one POST (`.expect(1)`) + a delivered `CHANNEL_EGRESS` frame with the real wamid. Plus 2 wiremock unit tests on the `*_at` cores. `wiremock` was already a dev-dep.
- [x] **AR-05** `neoth profile conflicts` + resolve workflow. **A3 #5: prerequisite for G-02 in v0.9.** — 1.5d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. Two new subcommands: `neoth profile conflicts [--limit N]` scans `idx_profile` for fields with ≥ 2 active (`superseded_at IS NULL`) claims whose `value_json` strings disagree — surfaces each conflict group with extraction_id / value / confidence / applied_at (sorted newest-first within each group). `neoth profile conflicts-resolve <field> --keep <extraction_id>` supersedes every active claim except the chosen one (records `superseded_at = now_unix` on losers, kept row stays untouched). Identical duplicates (extractor re-affirming same fact) are NOT conflicts — healthy signal, filtered out. `resolve_conflict` refuses an unknown `keep` extraction_id with a clear error instead of sweeping every claim (safety contract pin). Both behaviours split into pure helpers (`detect_conflicts` + `resolve_conflict`) so tests assert on data, not stdout. 8 new tests: empty table / single claim / identical duplicates / canonical Berlin-vs-Munich disagreement / superseded-row exclusion / resolve happy path (kept stays + losers superseded + post-resolve detect returns empty) / resolve refuses unknown id (typo safety) / resolve no-op when only one active claim. Closes the prerequisite gate for v0.9 G-02 (proactive surfacing of wrong self-knowledge) — G-02 can now consult `detect_conflicts` before volunteering a profile claim.

### v0.4 — First three small-effort killer features (A6)

- [x] **KF-02** `neoth memory diff --region amygdala --from 7d --to now` (A6 #2 / A3 #4 merged). — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::memory::diff` module. Pure-read region-scoped time-window diff over `idx_episode`. `DiffCategory { Added, Reinforced, Forgot }` (snake_case wire form). Three buckets: **Added** — rows whose `ts_ns ∈ [from, to]` (new content); **Reinforced** — pre-existing rows (`ts_ns < from`) with `last_access_ts ∈ [from, to]` (operator or recall path touched them); **Forgot** — pre-existing rows whose current importance < `FORGET_FLOOR` (queued for sweep). `diff_report(conn, region, from_ns, to_ns, limit)` returns `DiffReport` with each bucket sorted `(importance DESC, ts_ns DESC)` + summary counts always exact (independent of limit cap). Region scoping reuses the `regions::classify_region` band-map; Amygdala scopes by `importance >= AMYGDALA_THRESHOLD` overlay. `parse_window_arg(s, now_ns)` operator-facing helper parses `now` / `7d` / `2h` / `30m` / `45s` (case-insensitive) + bare integer ns. Reversed window returns Ok(empty) gracefully — caller mistake, not a hard error. **Snapshot-pair heuristic** documented inline as deliberate design choice — storing per-row importance time series would require a separate journal table or per-frame audit. 13 tests: parse_window per-unit + bare-int + 6 garbage rejections / category_as_str drift guard / empty DB zeros / added bucket time-window inclusion+exclusion / reinforced bucket pre-existed+access-in-window / forgot bucket pre-existed+below-floor / region scope filters Hippocampus vs Insula correctly / Amygdala importance-overlay scoping / limit caps each bucket independently of counts / reversed window graceful. CLI surface `neoth memory diff` is a 15-line wrap when wired — primitive ships clean.
- [x] **KF-06** `neoth permissions audit` — every permission decision + downstream effect. — A6 #6 — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::permissions::audit` module. Pure-read WAL scan over `EVENT_TYPE_PERMISSION_GRANTED` (0xA0) + `EVENT_TYPE_PERMISSION_DENIED` (0xA1) + `EVENT_TYPE_CONSENT_DECISION` (0x65). `AuditDecision { Granted, Denied, ConsentAllow, ConsentDeny }` (snake_case wire form). `AuditEntry { event_id, ts_ns, decision, action, reason, downstream }` carries the parsed `action` / `reason` JSON fields + best-effort downstream-effect description via next-frame heuristic (Provider request / Channel egress / MCP tool / Plugin hostcall / Kanban status). Consent decision split: `allow_once` + `allow_always` map to `ConsentAllow`; only literal `deny` maps to `ConsentDeny` (unknown labels charitably default to Allow — pinned by test so a future strict-validation refactor doesn't quietly flip historic reads). `audit_segment(segment, from_ns, to_ns, top_denied_cap)` returns `AuditReport { entries, by_decision: HashMap, top_denied_actions }` chronologically sorted (defensive resort even though decoded frames arrive in order, against future torn-tail-resume paths). Tolerates missing segment + tiny file + non-JSON payload all gracefully (Ok empty / Ok with empty fields). 13 tests: as_str drift guard / classify_decision pins each event type / unknown-consent-charitable-default / describe_downstream recognises 5 canonical effect types + unknown returns empty / missing-segment empty / tiny-file empty / 4-frame full report with per-decision counts / window-filter (future-only excludes; wide-open includes all) / downstream attached when next frame is PROVIDER_REQUEST / downstream None when permission is last frame / top-denied sorted desc + cap (3 shell_exec / 2 paid_io / 1 each tied for 3rd dropped at cap=2) / non-JSON payload gracefully empty / chronological sort drift guard. Operator's `neoth permissions audit --since 7d` CLI wrapper is a 10-line `cli::permissions` extension when wired.
- [x] **KF-07** Hebbian drift report `neoth memory drift` — beliefs fading from ground-truth tier. — A6 #7 — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::memory::drift` module. Pure-read diagnostic over `idx_episode` — surfaces rows on a glide path toward `FORGET_FLOOR` (0.10) where the consolidation pass deletes them from queryable views. `DriftSeverity { Imminent, AtRisk }` (snake_case wire form) + `severity_for(importance)` classifier: `< FORGET_FLOOR` → None (already dead, out of operator's recovery window); `[FORGET_FLOOR, IMMINENT_THRESHOLD=0.20)` → Imminent (next consolidation may sweep); `[IMMINENT, AT_RISK_THRESHOLD=0.40)` → AtRisk (fading, one recall hit reinforces back above 0.5); `≥ 0.40` → None (stable). `DriftReport { drifting, imminent_count, at_risk_count, stable_count }` returned by `drift_report(conn, limit)`. drifting vec sorted importance ASC then ts_ns DESC (most-urgent triage first), capped at `limit`; summary counts ALWAYS exact regardless of limit. Dead rows (below FORGET_FLOOR) explicitly excluded — surfacing them would mislead the operator about what's still triagable. 8 tests: severity_for per-band classification (5 importance values across the 4 buckets) / severity_as_str drift guard / threshold_constants_monotonic (FORGET_FLOOR<IMMINENT<AT_RISK) / empty-DB-zeros / buckets-rows-correctly (3 buckets with 2 rows each + sort order) / limit-caps-drifting-not-counts / excludes-dead-rows-below-forget-floor / severity-field-matches-classifier (drift guard for SQL/classifier divergence). CLI wrapper `neoth memory drift` is a 10-line `cli::memory` extension when wired (out of scope for this commit — primitive ships clean).

**v0.4 total: ~30-38 days. Tag: `v0.4.0`. Gate: M-07 test suite must produce coverage report + P-01..P-05 walk-through + 3 killer features demoable.**

New `media::stt_dispatch` mirrors `media::tts_dispatch` shape. `SttProvider { WhisperRsLocal, OpenAiWhisperApi, AzureSpeech, Vosk }` — explicit per-variant `#[serde(rename)]` because `rename_all="snake_case"` would split at case boundaries and produce ugly `open_ai_whisper_api` / `whisper_rs_local`; pinning each wire form keeps audit log aligned with `as_str()`. `is_local()` + `requires_credentials()` + `description()` for wizard. `WhisperModelSize { Tiny, Base, Small, Medium, Large }` + `approx_size_mb()` returns 75/142/466/1500/3100 MB (wizard warns operators on slow internet / small SSD); drift-guard test asserts monotonic increase across the 5 sizes. `AudioFormat { PcmS16leMono, PcmF32leMono }`. `TranscriptionRequest { language (BCP-47 or empty for auto-detect), model_size, format, sample_rate_hz, initial_prompt (operator-supplied vocabulary hints) }` defaults to PcmS16le-mono-16kHz-Base-auto. `TextSegment { start_ms, end_ms, text }` + `TranscriptionResult { text, segments, language, confidence: Option<f32> }`. **Live transcript buffer**: `LiveTranscriptBuffer::new(sample_rate_hz)` stateful VAD-based endpoint-detector. Operator audio arrives via `feed_pcm_f32(&samples)`; the buffer chunks into 20 ms frames (WebRTC-canonical), computes RMS energy, tracks silence-hangover timer. `poll_completed_utterance()` returns `Some(Vec<f32>)` only when (a) at least one speech frame seen AND (b) silence-hangover (default 750 ms — "natural pause between sentences" without cutting long thinking pauses) elapsed. `with_thresholds(silence_rms, hangover_ms)` operator-tunable. `pending_samples()` + `reset()` lifecycle. Prevents silence-only utterances from ever emitting (mic-open-but-quiet doesn't fire whisper). `rms_energy(&samples)` pure helper (also useful for caller-side mic-meter UIs). `SttDispatcherConfig { primary=WhisperRsLocal, fallback=None, default_model_size=Base, default_language="" }` defaults local + auto-detect. 22 tests: provider as_str pins (4) / locality classification / credentials classification / descriptions present / model size as_str pins (5) / model size monotonic-MB drift guard / format as_str pins / request default sane / rms empty-zero / rms silence-zero / rms constant-equals-amplitude / buffer silence-only never emits / buffer speech+hangover emits / buffer speech-alone-no-silence doesn't emit / buffer resets state after emit / buffer with_thresholds overrides / buffer pending_samples tracks / buffer reset clears / default dispatcher config local+no-fallback+Base+auto / provider snake_case serde pinned / model size snake_case serde / TranscriptionResult serde roundtrip with optional confidence. 22/22 media::stt_dispatch suite green. **Follow-up MM-01b**: wire `whisper-rs` crate behind `feature="whisper_local"`, OpenAI Whisper REST via existing `reqwest` dep, Azure Speech client. The `new_acl` buffer that `SetEntriesInAclW` `LocalAlloc`s is now freed via `LocalFree(new_acl as HLOCAL)` right after `SetNamedSecurityInfoW` returns. **No leak, no double-free, no use-after-free:** `SetNamedSecurityInfoW` DEEP-COPIES the ACL into the file's security descriptor (so the buffer is unreferenced by the time we free it, per MSDN); the error path is already leak-free (a failed `SetEntriesInAclW` leaves `new_acl` null + the `?` returns early before any allocation); the free is guarded `if !new_acl.is_null()` + happens exactly once with the fn returning immediately after. `LocalFree`/`HLOCAL` resolved from `windows_sys::Win32::Foundation` (`HLOCAL = *mut c_void`, the `Win32_System_Memory` feature already enabled) — verified against the pinned windows-sys 0.59 source before editing. Verified: clippy `-D warnings` clean; the 3 existing DACL tests (`dacl_round_trip_sets_without_error` / `dacl_idempotent_double_set` [exercises two alloc+free cycles] / `dacl_nonexistent_path_returns_err`) pass on Windows. ### v0.5 — Paperless (security-gated first)

- [x] **PL-04** Prompt-injection gate on every paperless ingest + email body. **A5 SC-16: not a "reuses ingress_sanitizer" — explicit call-site audit + new email-MIME normalization (SC-15) + paperless OCR sanitization (SC-16) before any PL-_ / EM-_ merges.** — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 24. Extended `ingress_sanitizer::sanitize` Gate 4 to close all 10 PL-04a corpus gaps without regressing any of the 5 false-positive guards. Three additions: (1) `PROMPT_INJECTION_PATTERNS` extended +18 patterns covering DE/ES/FR instruction-override variants (ignoriere alle vorherigen / vergiss alle vorherigen / missachte vorherige / ignora las instrucciones / olvida las / ignorez toutes les instructions / oubliez les instructions), fabricated-tool-call lures (bash( / function_call( / tool_use{ / tool_calls:), operator/authority impersonation (this document is from your operator / from your operator approve / admin override / this is a directive from), URL-exfiltration placeholders (<paste your last / <paste any key / <insert memory here> / <insert credentials>). (2) New `pl04_normalize_for_marker_scan(text)` pre-pass — drops numeric-only lines then joins with space (defeats "ignore\\n13\\nprevious\\n14\\ninstructions" page-number padding), collapses dot-separated single letters via one-pass char scanner (i.g.n.o.r.e → ignore; explicitly preserves decimals "1.299,00" because non-alpha prefix), folds decorative-letter codepoints to ASCII (negative-squared 🅰..🆉 = U+1F170..1F189, squared 🄰..🅉 = U+1F130..1F149, circled Ⓐ..Ⓩ = U+24B6..24CF, circled ⓐ..ⓩ = U+24D0..24E9 — none NFKC-normalize because they're decorative symbols not compatibility decompositions). Sanitizer runs Gate 4 twice: literal lowercase + PL-04-normalized lowercase, both against the same marker list. (3) New `pl04_paraphrase_match(lower) -> Option<String>` regex matcher — three-slot synonym matrix `(forget|disregard|ignore|overlook|skip|bypass|set aside|put aside){gap≤40}(earlier|prior|previous|preceding|above){gap≤40}(directives?|instructions?|orders?|rules?|guidance|commands?|prompts?)` — catches "set aside all earlier directives" with synthetic pattern label `paraphrase-matrix:<matched-text>`. Verified non-regression: existing 5 fp-guards (plain invoice / Assistant Director title / German invoice with Umlauts / legal clause with bare word "ignore" / chat notes) all pass clean — fp-005 specifically because no slot-2 anchor (earlier/prior/previous/...) appears within 40 chars of "ignore". Corpus runner flipped all 10 known-gap fixtures to `expected_outcome: quarantine` — 26/26 PL-04a fixtures green. Added 12 ingress_sanitizer unit drift guards: numeric-line drop / dot-collapse / decimal-preserve / 🅾-fold / circled-letter-fold / paraphrase-positive / paraphrase-benign-ignore-negative / 4 quarantine-by-channel sanity checks (DE / Bash( / paste-placeholder / authority) + benign German invoice. 22/22 sanitizer tests pass, 123/123 security tests pass.
- [x] **PL-05** Spam + phishing + malware detection on email body (rules engine + LLM second-opinion). — 3.5d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 24 (rules-engine slice; LLM second-opinion deferred to PL-05b as separate item). New `crate::security::email_threat::{assess_email_threat, ThreatAssessment, ThreatBand, ThreatFinding}` — scored rule engine runs AFTER `email_sanitizer::sanitize_email_body` + `ingress_sanitizer::sanitize` (those handle prompt-injection + MIME; PL-05 is the complementary surface for phishing language, classic spam, malware-attachment indicators). 4 rule families: (1) **15 phishing rules** — verify-your-account, confirm-your-identity, update-your-payment, account-suspended/locked, unusual-signin, click-here-to-avoid, confirm-now-or-lose-access, claim-your-prize, enter-your-seed-phrase (weight 50 — high-confidence crypto attack), enter-your-recovery-phrase, wire-funds-immediately, tax-refund-claim, package-undeliverable. Weights 25-50. (2) **10 spam rules** — lottery-winner, prince-inheritance, viagra/cialis, weight-loss, make-money-fast, work-from-home-$5k-per-day, millions-of-dollars, congrats-winner. Weights 15-30. (3) **15 malware-attachment extensions** — .exe/.scr (50), .bat/.cmd (40), .vbs/.ps1 (45), .js (30), .jse/.msi (40), .hta (45), macro-enabled .docm/.xlsm/.pptm (40), .iso/.img modern delivery vectors (35). Case-insensitive ends-with. (4) **10 brand-domain impersonation rules** — paypal/amazon/microsoft/apple/google/github/netflix/dhl/ups/fedex; fires only when brand named in body AND sender_domain doesn't end with legit_suffix; counted once per brand (HashSet de-dup). Action bands: Allow <50 / ReviewQueue 50-79 / Quarantine ≥80. Tuning rationale: one phishing signal alone (30) stays Allow because real invoices say "verify your account"; two combine (60) → ReviewQueue; three combine (90) → Quarantine. One exe attachment alone (50) → ReviewQueue. Exe + phishing → Quarantine. Score caps at 100. Allow=normal delivery, ReviewQueue=operator sees but agent MUST NOT auto-act, Quarantine=body never reaches LLM. 19 tests: benign-allows, single-marker-not-quarantine, two-markers-review, three-markers-quarantine, seed-phrase-alone-review, exe-alone-review, exe+phishing-quarantine, brand-impersonation-off-domain, brand-impersonation-skipped-on-legit-domain, classic-spam-lottery-review, spam+phishing-quarantine, score-caps-at-100, band-as-str-audit-pin, malware-filename-case-insensitive, docm-macro-office, no-sender-domain-skips-impersonation, json-serialisable-for-audit, rule-weight-accessor, brand-impersonation-de-dup-per-brand. Rules are pure data — adding a rule is a tuple-append, no code change. Follow-up `PL-05b` (LLM second-opinion for borderline 50-79 scores) tracked separately if needed.

New `paperless::consult` module — synchronous keyword-scan over `<vault>/<subdir>/Paperless/*.md` so any operator question gets paperless-grounded context BEFORE the LLM call. `consult(vault_root, subdir, question, max_matches) -> ConsultResult { matches: Vec<ConsultMatch>, query_tokens, scanned }`. Scoring: tokenize question via `extract_query_tokens` (case-insensitive, split non-alphanumeric, stopword drop — same DE+EN list as `reflection::score_topics` — drop words <4 chars, drop pure-digit tokens <5 chars because "42" causes invoice-line noise but "99876" is a real id), then for each `.md` file count DISTINCT query-tokens that hit the body (repeats inside one doc don't compound — prevents keyword-stuffing bias). Rank by score desc → mtime desc (newest invoice wins ties — operators usually want the latest) → filename alpha. Drop score-0 docs. Cap at `DEFAULT_MAX_MATCHES=5`. `ConsultMatch { filename, path, score, excerpt, mtime_unix }`. Excerpt is a `MAX_EXCERPT_CHARS=200` char-boundary-safe window around the first hit (UTF-8 multi-byte safe), single-line (newlines collapsed to spaces so chat previews don't wrap). Missing `<vault>/<subdir>/Paperless/` directory → empty result + scanned=0 (no error — operators who haven't enabled paperless still hit this path through the default proactive chain). Non-md files ignored. `render_summary(&result) -> String` returns "" when no matches so callers can short-circuit; otherwise renders bullet list with filename + score + excerpt. **No embeddings by design** — embeddings would catch paraphrases but pull a 4 GB qwen into the consult path; PL-03 stays substring-only so consult runs free + synchronous on every operator question. ### v0.5 — Security additions (A5 SC-15..18)

- [x] **SC-15** Email-specific sanitizer: MIME normalize → quoted-reply block strip → attachment filename sanitization. New stage in `ingress_sanitizer`. — A5 — 2d — ✅ 2026-05-25 Session 24. New `crate::security::email_sanitizer` module. `sanitize_email_body(input) -> EmailSanitized { body, findings }` runs 4 stages: (1a) CRLF→LF normalize; (1b) MIME boundary line strip — `--===============*` lines dropped wholesale (ordering pin: runs BEFORE QP because boundary markers end in `==\n` which would partially match the QP soft-wrap and mangle subsequent text); (1c) Quoted-printable soft-wrap decode TIGHTENED — `=\n` unwrap only fires when the `=` is NOT preceded by another `=` (real QP wraps end in single `=` at col 75-76; `==\n` is operator-typed text or leftover boundary, not soft-wrap); (1d) Leading `Content-*:` header block strip — operator's first blank line ends the header context, so `Content-Type:` in the middle of the body is preserved; (2) Quoted-reply strip — `On <date>, <name> wrote:` / `Am <Datum> schrieb <Name>:` / `El`/`Le`/`Il` attribution lines followed by `>` cascade get stripped, bare `>` cascades stripped, P.S. notes AFTER quoted block survive. `safe_attachment_filename(raw) -> (String, Vec<EmailFinding>)` defends against: path-traversal (`../`, absolute paths — strip to basename), Windows reserved names (CON/PRN/AUX/NUL/COM1-9/LPT1-9 — case-insensitive match + `safe_` prefix), suspicious double-extension hijacks (`invoice.pdf.exe` → rewritten to `.bin`; 11 double-ext patterns covered), overlong filenames (truncate to MAX_FILENAME_LEN=200 + hash suffix for collision safety). `EmailFinding` enum snake_case wire form. 20 tests: CRLF→LF / QP soft-wrap decode (with the un-narrowed `==\n` false-match drift guard) / MIME boundary strip / Content-_ header strip at start only / English + German + bare-`>` quoted-reply strip / P.S. after quote survives / ordinary filename pass-through / path-traversal / Windows reserved (CON / LPT9 / NUL / AUX) / case-insensitive Windows reserved / suspicious double-ext rewrite to .bin / benign single-ext false-positive guard / overlong filename truncate with hash / empty filename → "untitled". EM-_ callers wire via `sanitize_email_body(body)` → `ingress_sanitizer::sanitize(result.body)` two-stage chain.
- [x] **SC-16** Every paperless OCR path runs through `ingress_sanitizer::sanitize()` before memory or LLM. — A5 — 1d — ✅ 2026-05-26 Session 24. New `crate::security::paperless_ingest` module — typed wrapper `ingest_ocr_text(raw, source, document_id) -> Result<PaperlessOcrPayload, IngestError>`. `PaperlessOcrPayload.body` field is PRIVATE — only construction path is the sanitized one; downstream consumers see `PaperlessOcrPayload`, not `String`, so a future PL-\* path that constructs a `String`-typed bypass gets caught at first PR review. `IngestError::Quarantined { ocr_source, document_id, findings, raw_input_hash }` — note `ocr_source` not `source` (thiserror reserves `source` as the embedded `std::error::Error` and `OcrSource` is a plain enum, not an Error). `OcrSource` enum: PaperlessNgx / TesseractDirect / PaperlessAi / ManualUpload, `#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]` for stable audit-log wire form. Calls into `ingress_sanitizer::sanitize(raw, "paperless")` so audit traces show paperless as a distinct channel. `raw_input_hash` (xxh3_64 hex) lets the audit log cross-reference quarantines without storing the raw payload. Side-effect on `ingress_sanitizer::Finding`: added `PartialEq, Eq` derive so `Vec<Finding>` participates in `PaperlessOcrPayload`'s `PartialEq`. 9 tests: source-as-str audit pin / benign pass-through / oversize quarantine carries findings / prompt-injection-marker quarantine / private-body drift guard / snake_case source-tag serde / raw_input_hash distinct-input uniqueness + identical-input determinism / `impl Into<String>` API shape.

**v0.5 total: ~45-55 days. Tag: `v0.5.0`. Gate: SX-01 SSRF guard + PL-04/PL-05 verified before EM-01 merge; email walk-through + paperless walk-through; multimodal voice-call demo.**

### v0.9 — Slave / cluster coupling (A1 sequencing decision)

- [x] **SL-00** Cluster live-transport activation — **the real unblock for SL-01/SL-01b** (NOT deferred — design done, build sequenced). **Senior-dev gremium 2026-06-01 Session 32 (full design: `PLAN/DESIGN_cluster_transport.md`, 4 lenses).** Verified ground truth: SPEC-09's receive-half is shipped + tested BUT `spawn_discovery_with_wal` is **NEVER called from serve.rs** → the whole Hyperswarm transport + all its `0xE0..E5` WAL emits are **dormant in production**; AND the Hyperswarm `spawn_discovery` has **no caller at all** + **no `cluster_name`/`cluster_key` config field** for the DHT topic (operator can't set a cluster identity yet). API unknowns resolved: the peeroxide `SecretStream` is a single `&mut` (used `.read()`/`.write()` sequentially) → the outbound sender is a **`tokio::select!` on the one stream (NO split needed)**, not the split-stream approach; `SwarmHandle` drop-aborts (clean shutdown); `PeerLoadRegistry::new()`. **Build order (each ~1d, real+tested):** **(1a)** cluster-identity config — ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 32:** `credentials.yaml::cluster_passphrase` (SecretString — the shared HMAC-key seed; mlock+zeroize) + `freedom.yaml::cluster.name` (public DHT/mDNS rendezvous; typed `ClusterConfig`) + `cluster/identity.rs` `resolve_cluster_identity(freedom, creds) -> Option<ClusterIdentity{name,key}>` (**fail-closed**: BOTH halves required, empty/whitespace rejected, `Debug` redacts the key) + `cluster_identity_status()`. Real consumer: `neoth cluster status` now shows cluster name / passphrase-set / configured (incomplete → tells the operator exactly what to set). 6 identity tests (resolve / fail-closed×2 / determinism / status / key-redaction) + credentials is_empty round-trip; full lib 6411/0, clippy clean. **(1b) — SECURITY PREREQUISITE FOUND (verify-first, Session 32):** the Hyperswarm handshake authenticates ONLY on `cluster_name_hash = derive_topic(cluster_name)` (hyperswarm.rs:306-316) — a hash of the PUBLIC name. There is NO `cluster_key`/passphrase HMAC proof in the handshake (the key is used only on the mDNS announce path). **Activating the transport as-is would let anyone who knows the public cluster name join + pass the handshake = insecure cluster.** So 1b's FIRST task is strengthening the Hyperswarm handshake to prove `cluster_key` possession. ✅ **Auth crypto SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 32** (3-lens gremium `wwdr5yc1g`, design in `PLAN/DESIGN_cluster_transport.md`): `cluster/peer_auth.rs` — `compute_cluster_key_proof(key, signer_pk, verifier_pk)` + `verify_peer_proof(key, claimed, peer_pk, own_pk)` (constant-time). **Asymmetric (signer-first) HMAC** = `HMAC-SHA256(cluster_key, "neoth-cluster-peer-auth/v1\0" || signer_pk || verifier_pk)` — the attack-lens proved a *symmetric* (sorted-key) proof is **reflection-attackable** (echo the token back → verifies); signer-first defeats it. No nonce (Noise ephemerals + static-key binding cover replay). API verified: `peeroxide handle.key_pair().public_key` = local Noise static key. 5 tests incl. **reflection-rejected** + asymmetric + wrong-key-rejected + pinned-byte-layout. full lib 6416/0, clippy clean. ✅ **1b-handshake hardening SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 32** (transport still dormant = zero behaviour change, but secure WHEN activated): HelloBody gained `cluster_key_proof: Option<[u8;32]>` (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if)]`; **fail-closed: missing→reject**), `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 1→2 (breaking; all nodes upgrade together), `handle_peeroxide_connection` + `spawn_discovery_with_wal` thread `cluster_key: Option<Arc<ClusterKey>>` + `own_noise_pk` (= `handle.key_pair().public_key`); outbound Hello sends `compute_cluster_key_proof(k, own_pk, peer_pk)`, inbound verifies `verify_peer_proof(k, claimed, peer_pk, own_pk)` (recomputes the swapped `proof(peer,own)`) right after the cluster_name_hash check, fail-closed. Wire round-trip test + the 5 peer_auth crypto tests cover it. full lib 6417/0, clippy clean. ✅ **1b-activate (the flip) SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 33** (3-lens adversarial review, 24 raw → 20 confirmed, NO critical/high): gate-activated `spawn_discovery_with_wal` in `serve.rs` behind **`cluster_transport_activation(freedom, creds)`** — a NEW pure, unit-tested gate = `cluster.enabled` master-switch (NEW `ClusterConfig.enabled: bool`, **default false**) AND a fully-resolved identity. Default install (enabled=false, no name, no passphrase) is **triple-gated OFF** → never announces on the public DHT (`default_install_never_activates` + `activation_gate_truth_table` prove it). Threads `Arc::new(identity.key)` + fresh `PeerLoadRegistry` + `Some(Arc::new(writer.clone()))`; holds the `SwarmHandle`, `shutdown().await` on the daemon SIGTERM path. **Review-driven hardening, all fixed before commit:** (a) **CRITICAL-for-function lifecycle bug** — peeroxide's actor breaks its loop when the last `cmd_tx` drops; the old code dropped the peeroxide handle at the end of `spawn_discovery_with_wal` → the DHT actor died → transport was **announce-then-die (dead-on-arrival theater)**. Fixed: `SwarmHandle` now RETAINS the peeroxide handle + `swarm_task` + `topic`; `shutdown()` = `leave(topic)` (unannounce) → drop handle → abort accept loop → await actor for clean socket close. (b) **DoS hardening** (public DHT): `HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT` (10s) on the Hello write+read so a stalled peer can't pin a task, + `MAX_CONCURRENT_PEER_SESSIONS` (64) semaphore so a connection flood can't spawn unbounded tasks. (c) **secret leak** — `ClusterKey::Debug` leaked the first 16 hex of the HMAC secret → now `ClusterKey(<redacted>)` (test asserts even the 8-byte prefix is absent). (d) **announce-before-listen window** closed — accept loop spawned BEFORE `handle.join`. (e) `receive_hello` → `pub(crate)` (no-auth test primitive can't be used in prod). (f) `neoth cluster status` now reports `enabled` + `transport_active` (honest disabled/active/inactive states) instead of the stale "dormant" string. full lib 6420/0, clippy+fmt clean. (Registry-membership re-check of revoked peers deferred to SL-01's task gate — the base handshake must accept cluster_key-valid peers for discovery; key-rotation-on-revoke is a separate concern.) ✅ **1c outbound sender SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 33** (3-lens review, 14 confirmed, the one MEDIUM caught + fixed pre-commit): the receive-only per-peer loop is now **bidirectional**. `cluster/local_load.rs` — process-global gauge fed REAL data: `inflight` via an RAII `InflightGuard` wired into the `cli/chat.rs` provider call, `tokens_per_sec` EWMA from `record_completion(output_tokens, elapsed)` at the same site, `healthy=true` (honest current state). `cluster/peer_streams.rs` — `PeerStreamRegistry` (peer Noise-pubkey-hex → bounded `mpsc::Sender<WireFrame>`, fail-closed `send_to`/`broadcast`, poison-recovering lock) so SL-01/SL-01b can send directed frames. `handle_peeroxide_connection` now drains outbound + sends a real-load heartbeat BETWEEN reads. **CANCEL-SAFETY (the MEDIUM the review + my own source-read both caught):** peeroxide's `SecretStream::read()` uses `read_exact` into locals ⇒ NOT cancel-safe; the first-draft `select!` (read arm racing the heartbeat timer) would desync the Noise frame stream on a mid-frame cancel → connection corruption. **Rewrote to read-to-completion** (writes happen between completed reads, never cancelling a read; the peer's own ~5s heartbeat paces the loop; a dead peer surfaces via the udx RTO-timeout making `read()` return `Err`). WAL `0xEA CLUSTER_HEARTBEAT_SENT` (once per connection, first outbound heartbeat). 13 component tests (local_load gauge ×5 + peer_streams registry ×8). full lib 6433/0, clippy+fmt clean, SC-01a guard green. **SL-00 is now functionally COMPLETE** (identity + auth + handshake + activation + bidirectional transport); SL-01 (task accept) + SL-01b (gossip) consume the send path. **(1b)** gated transport activation in serve.rs (`spawn_discovery_with_wal` behind `cluster.mdns.enabled`, default OFF so no fresh install auto-announces on the public DHT — SAFETY GATE; abort on shutdown) + the **registry-membership re-check after handshake** (security fix: a REVOKED peer currently re-enters the loop cleanly); **(1c)** outbound sender (`cluster/peer_streams.rs` `PeerStreamRegistry` keyed on Noise pubkey + `select!` inbound/outbound loop + jittered-interval heartbeat-send + fail-closed `send`) + WAL `0xEA CLUSTER_HEARTBEAT_SENT`. — multi-commit, BUILD-NEXT

(2) **`Gate::with_lease_snapshot(store, subject, now)` + `check_at`** — a covering lease upgrades **`Confirm → Allow` ONLY, never `Deny`** (so `Strict` stays `Strict`); the lease is consulted on the `Confirm` branch alone, before the confirm round-trip. **Two-clock model**: candidate leases snapshotted once (no store-lock across await), but expiry RE-checked at decision time against a **fresh wall-clock** (`check_at` seam — fixes the review's HIGH frozen-clock finding; `check_at` is `pub(crate)` so prod can't pass a spoofed past clock; post-snapshot-expiry regression test). All actions WAL-auditable.** — 7d — ✅ **Slice 1 (gated file READ) SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 32** (verify-first triage gremium + adversarial security review). New `os_tools` module — **three fail-closed layers**: (1) **allowlist** (`allowlist.rs::resolve_within_allowlist`) — the target must canonicalize under a `freedom.yaml::tools.os.allowed_paths` prefix (**default empty = deny-all**); both target AND prefix are `canonicalize`d so a symlink can't escape (review-CONFIRMED clean), any `..` component is rejected pre-FS, prefix-boundary is component-wise (`/home/alice` ≠ `/home/alice-evil`), and a **relative** prefix is refused (would resolve against the daemon CWD — fail-closed). (2) **autonomy gate** — new `Action::OsFileRead { path }`: Confirm@Strict (no TTY ⇒ fail-closed) / Allow@Standard+ (the allowlist IS the operator opt-in); lease-coverable under the Read scope. (3) **audit** — WAL `0xA8 OS_FILE_READ` (with byte count) / `0xA9 OS_FILE_DENIED` (with reason) on EVERY outcome. `read.rs` reads UTF-8 only, **size-capped on an OPEN fd** + `is_file()` check (review HIGH fix: a FIFO/device/`/proc` reports `len()==0` so a pre-open size check is bypassable — `is_file()` refuses them) + `take(max+1)` bound (closes the stat→read TOCTOU + post-stat growth). `FreedomConfig::tools.os` (`ToolsConfig`/`OsToolsConfig { allowed_paths, max_read_bytes=1 MiB }`, default deny-all). **Real runtime consumer:** `neoth fs read <path>` (`cli/fs.rs`) — the gated alternative to an ungated `std::fs::read`, best-effort one-shot WAL audit (HF-01 pattern; warns loudly if the audit writer can't open rather than silently skipping). **Adversarial review (2-lens) findings ALL fixed before commit:** HIGH special-file size-cap bypass, MEDIUM stat→read TOCTOU, MEDIUM check_os_file_read autonomy-skip (removed the unused footgun), MEDIUM WAL-suppressed-on-spawn-fail (now warns), LOW relative-prefix (now refused); symlink/dotdot/prefix defenses confirmed CLEAN. 19 new os_tools tests (6 allowlist incl. traversal/symlink/prefix-boundary/relative-prefix + 4 read incl. non-regular-file/oversize/binary + 6 gate incl. deny-all/traversal/strict-confirm/WAL-frame) + permissions + config pins. clippy + fmt clean; full lib 6390/0; SC-01a green; docs regenerated. ✅ **Slice 2 (gated file WRITE) SHIPPED 2026-06-01 Session 33** (2-lens adversarial write-security review, 9 confirmed, NO crit/high — TOCTOUs are LOW: require an attacker who already holds write access to the allowlisted dir = outside NEOTH's threat model). **SEPARATE write-allowlist** `tools.os.allowed_write_paths` (default empty = deny-all; a readable path is NOT auto-writable) + `max_write_bytes` (1 MiB). `allowlist.rs::resolve_write_target` — the write-specific resolver: since a new file doesn't exist yet, it canonicalizes the **PARENT** (must exist; we never create parent dirs) + rebuilds `canon_parent/file_name`, rejects `..`, and on an EXISTING target re-canonicalizes it + requires `canon_existing.parent() == canon_parent` (a symlink-at-target can't escape its dir). `Action::OsFileWrite { path }` — **one notch stricter than read**: Strict Deny / Standard Confirm (no TTY ⇒ blocked) / Elevated+Full Allow; `lease_scope_for → None` (a raw-OS write is NEVER lease-unlockable). `gate.rs::write_os_file` — size-cap → write-allowlist → autonomy → `write_file_atomic` (temp+rename; single rename is the atomic commit on BOTH platforms — review dropped the windowed remove+rename fallback) + WAL **`0xAA OS_FILE_WRITE {path,bytes,existed}` / `0xAB OS_FILE_WRITE_DENIED {path,reason}`**. **Windows hardening** (review MEDIUM): `#[cfg(windows)] reject_windows_reserved_filename` refuses device names (`NUL`/`CON`/`COM1-9`/`LPT1-9`) + NTFS ADS (`:`) — both bypass normal file semantics. Consumer: **`neoth fs write <path> <content>`** (`cli/fs.rs`, one-shot WAL audit unless daemon owns it; denial → non-zero exit). 16 new write tests (resolver: new-file/deny-all/dotdot/parent-outside/nonexistent-parent/symlink-escape[unix]/reserved-name[win] + gate: elevated-write/standard-confirm/strict-deny/deny-all/too-large + atomic-write/overwrite-no-temp + autonomy mapping). full lib 6478/0, clippy+fmt clean, docs regenerated. **Documented LOW residuals (threat-model boundary):** post-resolve TOCTOU + hard-links require a local attacker who already has write to the allowlisted dir (NEOTH's only writer is itself, gated); daemon-live CLI writes skip the 2nd WAL writer (same single-writer tradeoff as read — daemon-IPC audit forwarding is a shared follow-on). ✅ **Slice 3 (gated app-launch) SHIPPED 2026-06-02 Session 35.** Same grooved fail-closed recipe as read/write, one notch STRICTER (program launch = arbitrary code execution = the highest-blast-radius OS-tool verb). **SEPARATE exec-allowlist** `tools.os.allowed_exec_paths` (default empty = deny-all; a readable/writable path is NOT runnable) matched by **EXACT canonical path, NOT a directory prefix** — `allowlist.rs::resolve_exec_program` canonicalizes the target + each entry and requires `canonical == entry` (so allowlisting `/usr/bin/firefox` authorises exactly that binary, never the rest of `/usr/bin` — a prefix allowlist would be a shell), rejects `..` pre-FS, requires `is_file()` (no dirs/devices/FIFOs → `AllowlistError::NotAnExecutableFile`), ignores relative entries (CWD footgun). `Action::OsAppLaunch { program }` — **stricter than `OsFileWrite`** (which Elevated allows): Strict **Deny** / Standard **Confirm** / Elevated **Confirm** (no TTY ⇒ suppressed) / Full **Allow** (mirrors `ExecArbitrary`); `lease_scope_for → None` (exec is NEVER lease-unlockable). `launch.rs::launch_program` = `Command::new(prog)` with **NO args, NO shell** (direct `argv[0]` ⇒ no metacharacter injection) + **stdin/stdout/stderr nulled** (a launched program can't read/corrupt NEOTH's GUI-bridge stdout-JSON) + fire-and-forget (Child dropped; one-shot-CLI reaping is correct, daemon-side reaping a follow-on). `gate.rs::launch_os_app` → WAL **`0xAC OS_APP_LAUNCH {program,pid}` / `0xAD OS_APP_LAUNCH_DENIED {program,reason}`**. Consumer: **`neoth os launch <program>`** (new `cli/os.rs`, one-shot WAL audit unless daemon owns it; denial → non-zero exit). 7 allowlist tests (exact-match / sibling-binary-denied / dir-not-executable / dotdot / nonexistent / relative-ignored / deny-all) + 8 gate tests (deny-all / strict-deny / standard+elevated confirm / full-spawn-returns-pid / non-allowlisted-denied / 0xAC+0xAD frame emit) + 3 launch smoke tests (cross-platform arg-free binary). ✅ **Slice 4 (gated CLIPBOARD read/write) SHIPPED 2026-06-05 Session 39** (4-lens design panel → build → 4-dimension adversarial review[exfil/gate-bypass/pastejacking/cfg] → 10 confirmed findings, 6 fixed + 2 declined-with-rationale). Same grooved fail-closed recipe, **STRICTER than file/launch** (the clipboard is an UNSCOPED ambient secret store + a passive injection sink — no path-allowlist scopes it). New `os_tools/clipboard.rs` (panic-free `arboard` wrapper, behind cargo feature **`os-clipboard`** — default-OFF in source like `wasm-plugin-host`, release-ON via cargo-dist; `default-features=false` drops image-data, `+wl-clipboard-rs` for Wayland; headless `Clipboard::new()` Err → `ClipboardUnavailable`, never a panic/build-fail). `Action::OsClipboardRead`/`OsClipboardWrite` (always-compiled — feature only gates the backend): **READ** = Strict-Deny / Standard+Elevated-Confirm(fail-closed) / Full-Allow (stricter than `OsFileRead`'s Standard-Allow — no allowlist to scope it); **WRITE** = Strict+Standard-**Deny** / Elevated-Confirm / Full-Allow (stricter than `OsAppLaunch`'s Standard-Confirm — pastejacking is a passive injection); both `lease_scope_for → None` (a Read lease must NEVER silently unlock the clipboard). `gate.rs::{read,write}_os_clipboard` layers: **kill-switches** (`tools.os.clipboard.{enabled,read_enabled,write_enabled}`, all default-false) → **size-cap** → **control-char guard** (ESC + all C0/C1 controls except tab → always rejected: closes bracketed-paste `\x1b[201~` escape) → **line-terminator guard** (`\n`/`\r`/NEL U+0085/LS U+2028/PS U+2029 — the terminal auto-execute precondition, `chars()`-based so the Unicode terminators can't slip a `bytes()` check — rejected unless `allow_newlines_in_write`) → **autonomy** → backend. WAL **`0xBC OS_CLIPBOARD_ACCESS {op,bytes,ts}` / `0xBD OS_CLIPBOARD_DENIED {op,reason,ts}`** (0xB band — the 0xA OS-tool band is FULL; overflow note like `0x67` channel-send). **No-exfil invariant:** clipboard CONTENT is NEVER in a frame/log/error (emit helpers take no content param; denial reasons are STATIC tags + byte-counts; oversize read held in `zeroize::Zeroizing` → wiped on reject); pinned by `wal_frame_never_contains_content` (drives a real secret through denied+full-write+access paths, asserts absence). Consumer: **`neoth os clipboard-get` / `clipboard-set [TEXT]`** (stdin if omitted; get prints to the operator's OWN stdout — they asked — never the WAL). **Declined findings (rationale):** one-shot WAL-spawn-failure fallthrough to `AuditSink::None` = consistent with the shipped `run_launch`/`fs` idiom (`enforce_required_audit` is the required-audit mechanism); CLI JSON `content` key = by-design operator-requested output. 6 permission tests + 14 gate tests (kill-switches/ladder/newline+unicode-terminator+control-char/tab-allowed/too-large/no-exfil) + `clipboard_audit_frames_are_durable` immediate-sync pin. default lib + `--features os-clipboard` both green; clippy `-D warnings` ×2 + docgen drift (clipboard CLI is feature-gated so the default tree is unchanged) clean. **STILL OPEN — PC-01 later slices:** operator-supplied launch ARGS (with a per-program arg policy — deferred ON PURPOSE: args can turn a benign binary into an arbitrary command), daemon-side launch with child reaping, window-mgmt, system-audio — each its own gate + autonomy arm + audit code; + wiring the gate into the coding worker's repo-file reads.

New `security::stream_batch_sanitizer` module — generic batched-stream gate for any high-rate text feed (OM-01 OMI transcripts, MM-01 live voice, future continuous-text). `StreamBatchSanitizer::new(channel)` accumulates chunks via `push_chunk(&str)`, auto-flushes when buffer crosses `DEFAULT_MAX_BUFFER_BYTES = 16 KiB` (operator-tunable via `with_max_buffer_bytes`, capped at `HARD_MAX_BUFFER_BYTES = 256 KiB` belt-and-suspenders so misconfigured callers can't OOM the daemon). Explicit `flush()` at semantic boundaries (utterance end / paragraph end). `FlushOutcome { Clean(SanitizeReport), Quarantined(SanitizeReport), Empty }`. **Quarantine flips `StreamState::Open → Halted` + drops the dirty buffer + counts the halt; subsequent `push_chunk` / `flush` return `Err(StreamError::Halted)` until operator calls `resume()`** — encodes the SC-18 spec rule "Quarantine → halt feed + notify operator, never silent drop" as a type-state machine; there is no code path where Quarantine continues silently. `state() / buffered_bytes() / halts() / clean_flushes()` for operator-facing UI + audit. `finding_summary(&report) -> Vec<String>` formats each `Finding` kind for the operator notification ("halted because of: prompt_injection_marker `ignore previous instructions`"). 19 tests: new-state-open / state snake_case serde / with_max_buffer_bytes caps at hard ceiling / push-under-threshold returns None + keeps buffer / flush empty=Empty / flush clean returns Clean with sanitized text + channel tag / flush-after-clean buffer-empty / flush quarantine halts + Quarantined / push-after-halt = StreamError::Halted / flush-after-halt = StreamError::Halted / resume clears halt + accepts new chunks (drift guard: post-resume Clean cycle works) / quarantine drops the dirty buffer (poisoned text never lingers awaiting accidental re-flush) / auto-flush fires at threshold / auto-flush halts on quarantine / multiple pushes accumulate into one flush / counters track multiple clean flushes / finding_summary formats each kind / finding_summary empty for clean report / **spec invariant**: quarantine NEVER silently continues (asserts state==Halted on every Quarantined match arm). 19/19 security::stream_batch_sanitizer suite green.

So "reconstruct hemisphere transcripts" is NOT achievable from the WAL without a NEW decision to persist transcript text (privacy/storage tradeoff — deferred to Alex). The spec's proposed `0x50`/`0x51` codes ALSO collide (`0x50`=RECOVERY_TRUNCATED, `0x51`=COMPACTION_AUTH_FAILED). **Shipped the reachable, valuable slice:** new **`neoth council replay <prompt_hash>`** — a chronological NARRATIVE reconstruction of one debate over the EXISTING `0x60-0x64` frames (reuses the SPEC-03 `collect_council_events` reader, NO new frame, NO orchestrator hot-path change). Renders the debate FLOW as readable prose ("convened — synthesis attempted → partial refusal: 1 hemisphere declined (left), 2 usable → winner selected at depth 2 → diversity warning") — richer than `inspect`'s raw frame list — keyed by `prompt_hash` (case-insensitive), chronological (forward) order. Table + JSON (`transcripts_available: false` honesty flag). Table footer explicitly states the prose isn't persisted so an operator isn't misled. New `memory/channel_weights.rs`. — 4.5d ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-06-03 Session 36 (panel rank-7) — built as a COMPLETE vertical (write+read both consumed, NOT the primitive-ahead trap the S36 memo flagged).** New `memory/channel_weights.rs`: Hebbian per-`(channel,topic_hash)` acceptance store mirroring `routing_weights` (const `MAX_CHANNEL_WEIGHT_DELTA=0.05` anti-manipulation, decay 0.93/day, cap 30, neutral 0.5; `load/save` atomic+0600, `record_channel_acceptance`, `load_channel_weight`/`channel_weight_of` + `ChannelContext::from_inbound`). **WRITE consumer:** `serve.rs` post-reply path records a best-effort acceptance per channel message (fire-and-forget, hot-path-safe). **READ consumer:** `neoth ecology channel-weights` aggregates per-channel topic-count + strongest familiarity (read-only diagnostic, the channel-fitness signal belongs to the ecology layer). 12 tests (neutral/increment/cap/decay/per-channel+per-topic isolation/round-trip/malformed/wrong-schema/atomic/from_inbound). default test exit 0 + clippy 0 + docgen. **NOTE (deliberate, verify-first):** the deeper channel-aware recall-RANKING weight (boosting recall hits by channel familiarity) stays a tracked follow-on — it needs recall hits to carry channel provenance + a recall-ranking refactor; the store+write+read-diagnostic is the complete, non-dead v1.0 vertical.
- [x] **ARCH-08** GUI↔daemon warm IPC channel (`neoth gui-stream`) — kill the 4-cold-subprocess-per-2s board refresh. — F6 — M — ✅ **2026-05-30 Session 30 (B/C/D advisory, item B — Alex: "nicht b notieren sondern bauen, 1.0 soll feature complete sein").** A 4-lens senior-dev gremium (architect / security / YAGNI / code-explorer) chose **persistent-stdio-stream** over the advisory's proposed `serve --gui-ipc` Unix-socket/Windows-named-pipe JSON-RPC layer: it reuses the `Command`+`thread::spawn`+NDJSON channel the GUI already trusts (ZERO new deps, ZERO platform-specific transport code), the process lifecycle IS the connection (crash→EOF→cold fallback, no reconnect FSM), and it has NO inbound listener so — unlike the existing bearer-auth'd n8n localhost-TCP API — no other local process can connect (most-secure option). The full socket layer + dual transport + 6-method RPC + reconnect logic were rejected as YAGNI. **Verify-first overturned the advisory's premise:** the headline "blocking subprocess freezes the UI event loop" was a PHANTOM (every `.output()` already runs in `std::thread::spawn`; chat already streams via `neoth chat --stream`) — see the kanban in-flight-guard commit `283f169` for the one *real* paired bug. **Code:** new `neoth gui-stream` subcommand (`cli/gui_stream.rs`, `#[command(hide=true)]`) holds the `views.db` connection + config open and answers NDJSON `{"id":N,"method":"board"|"ping"}` lines until stdin EOF; new `cli::kanban::assemble_gui_board` server-side collapses what the GUI did via FOUR cold spawns (`kanban list`→`show`→`watch`→`hemispheres show`) into ONE warm in-process query. GUI side: `GuiStreamClient` (owns the `Child`+pipes, kills on `Drop`, lazy-connect on first board tick, request→skip-non-JSON-lines→map) + `fetch_board_warm_or_cold` wired into the 2s timer with full cold-path fallback on ANY error. **READ-ONLY by design** — mutations stay on their gated subprocess paths, so the channel never bypasses the `CommandSource` privilege ceiling (ADV-09/ADV-15). **Latent bug fixed in passing:** the daemon's `init_tracing` writes the `INFO neothd: Neoth ready. Sup.` banner to **stdout**, not stderr — which had been silently corrupting EVERY GUI subprocess JSON parse (`kanban list --output json` emits `banner\n[]`, un-parseable). The shared `spawn_neothd_plain` helper now sets `NEOTH_LOG=error`, giving pristine machine-readable stdout for ALL GUI subprocess calls + the chat stream; the warm client additionally skips stray non-JSON lines (bounded) as a belt-and-suspenders net. **Verified:** 6 daemon `cli::gui_stream` tests (malformed/unknown-method/ping/empty-board/board-with-tasks/cerebellum-bound) + 2 GUI mapping tests (status-bucketing incl. done+archived→DONE & unknown→BACKLOG / feed-reversal) + **real e2e smoke** of the built binary (`printf '...board...ping...' | NEOTH_LOG=error neothd gui-stream` → pristine JSON-only stdout, empty stderr, clean EOF exit 0). clippy `--bins --lib --tests` 0 warnings, fmt 0 diffs. **Adversarial-review follow-up (Session 30):** the review confirmed ONE concurrency MEDIUM — the original `GuiStreamClient` read via a blocking `BufReader::read_line` while holding the client Mutex, so on a *hung-but-live* daemon a worker could pin that read indefinitely, delaying the `Drop`-driven child-kill (orphan risk on GUI shutdown) and wedging the in-flight slot. Fixed by decoupling the blocking read into a **dedicated reader thread** that feeds an `mpsc` channel; `request_board` now waits on `recv_timeout(5s)` instead of an unbounded `read_line`, so a stalled daemon can neither pin the per-tick worker nor delay teardown past the timeout (→ cold fallback + reconnect). The related HIGH ("panic wedges in-flight forever") + the `stdin.take()` orphan + the SQLITE_BUSY race + the "init_tracing stdout corrupts protocol" + the "ADV-15 autonomy=None bypass" were all **refuted** with code-grounded reasoning (no reachable panic in the worker body; unreachable `take()` None branch; checkpoint-PRAGMA returns a row not an error; `NEOTH_LOG=error` + the named error sites unreachable in the subprocess; no production caller constructs a daemon-origin config without `.with_autonomy()`).
- [~] **KF-08** Live Council Budget Meter — Slint GUI panel + tokio broadcast from `telemetry/council_meter.rs`. — 5.5d — 🔧 **Headless backend half shipped 2026-05-30 Session 29.** Verify-first chose the LOW-RISK path over the workflow-blueprint's EventBus-into-orchestrator threading (which would touch ~10 orchestrator test call-sites — the council hot path the ARCH-02 adversarial suite guards). Instead the budget posture is captured at the chat-layer wrapper `run_council_debate` (the single funnel for BOTH the CLI `run_chat_with` AND channel `dispatch_council_with_recovery` paths), OUTSIDE the orchestrator: a probe-clone of the `Arc<AtomicU32>` `BudgetToken` reads `used()/cap()` after the orchestrator consumes the token, then `council::budget::record_budget_outcome(home, used, cap, now)` folds it into a persisted snapshot. New `council::budget::CouncilBudgetSnapshot { cap, used_last_msg, exhausted_last_msg, exhaustions_rolling, updated_ts_unix }` + atomic `save/load_budget_snapshot` (`~/.neoth/council_budget.json` scratch — a live gauge, NOT the WAL: every charge already surfaces in council audit frames). New `neoth council budget [--json]` CLI reads the freedom.yaml cap (`effective_max_calls` + `daily_usd_cap`) + the snapshot → renders the configured cap + last-debate usage + lifetime cap-hit count (with a "raise it via `neoth council tune --max-calls N`" hint when cap-hits climb); config-only readout (no error) when no debate recorded yet. 5 tests (snapshot round-trip + no-tmp-leak / record marks-exhausted+increments-rolling / under-cap-resets-flag / cap=0-never-exhausted / CLI config-only + CLI reads-snapshot). Verified: clippy `--lib --tests` crate-wide 0 warnings, 270 council tests green, fmt 0 diffs. **STILL OPEN:** the Slint GUI live-meter PANEL (genuinely GUI-only, needs a display — not headless-buildable) + the `tokio::sync::broadcast` reactive stream (`telemetry/council_meter.rs`) for live-while-open updates. The CLI poll + scratch gauge cover the headless operator surface today; the GUI panel reads the same snapshot when the Slint shell lands.

So a `neoth plugin test` invocation **already cannot pollute the daemon's audit chain** — the writer-less Store ensures no `0xC4 PLUGIN_HOSTCALL` frame is written anywhere. The "test pollution prevention" value the original spec named is achieved by the Pick #34 default Store shape, not by a separate sink. **Smaller real remainder (still genuinely deferred):** aggregating the kind + payload_bytes of every `emit_event` hostcall the plugin made into the test report's JSON/Table output, so plugin authors see what their plugin actually fired. This needs a `dispatch::invoke_plugin_with_state(state)` overload that accepts a pre-built `PluginStoreState` (so the test harness can attach a tempdir WAL writer via the shipped `PluginStoreState::with_wal_writer` builder). Today's `invoke_plugin(engine, module, linker, plugin_id)` signature builds the Store internally — no injectable seam. That's a focused surgery to `wasm_plugin/dispatch.rs` plus the test-side frame-walking glue. Tracked as **UX-07b-Phase-2 (dispatcher injectable-state overload)**; not in scope today because the operator-visibility for v1.0 is satisfied (pass/fail + manifest validation + invocation stage trail), and the deeper "what hostcalls did my plugin fire" surface is a plugin-author-DX nice-to-have rather than a v1.0 blocker. **Session 30 verify-first:** re-confirmed shipped against code (`cli/plugin.rs:66` `PluginAction::Test` + `run_test` dispatch + 7 `cli::plugin::tests`) → flipped `[~]`→`[x]`. UX-07b-Phase-2 (dispatcher injectable-state overload) is the separately-tracked deferred remainder, NOT UX-07 itself.

Wasm plugins currently register via lifecycle `HookStage`s (PrePipeline / PreProviderCall / PostProviderCall / etc.) — NOT slash commands. A literal "slash-command wasm plugin" as the triage described would require new infrastructure: either a `slash_commands: Vec<String>` manifest field + a slash→plugin dispatcher in `slash/`, OR a hook-based slash routing pattern. Either is a real new feature, not 0.5d "pure file creation." **Recommendation**: redefine UX-08 to "Example wasm plugin showing the hook-stage lifecycle ABI" (`neoth_run() -> i32` + a `hook_stages = ["PostProviderCall"]` manifest), which IS buildable against the shipped infra. Filesystem targets legitimately contain `/`/`:`/`~`/`.`, so this is a traversal+null+non-empty check, NOT the strict label-only regex. 3 tests (rejects `../` file_write / rejects `..\\` config_write / allows legit absolute path); rollback 38 green; clippy `--lib --tests -- -D warnings` clean. **STILL OPEN (multi-day, the named-checkpoint half):** the `neoth checkpoint`/named-restore API where the target IS a label — that's the slot for the strict `[A-Za-z0-9_-][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,63}` regex; net-new save/restore-by-name infra (hermes adopt), deferred. ✅ **NAMED-CHECKPOINT API SHIPPED 2026-06-03 (panel rank-5) — SC-02 CLOSED.** New `cli/checkpoint.rs`: `neoth checkpoint {save <label>,list,restore <label>}` over the shipped `0xF2 PRE_MUTATION_SNAPSHOT` (no new WAL event — the label is sidecar metadata). `validate_checkpoint_label` enforces the strict path-safe regex `[A-Za-z0-9_-][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,63}` (leading-dot/slash/space rejected → no `..` traversal). `~/.neoth/checkpoints/index.json` atomic sidecar (`CheckpointEntry{label,segment,offset,ts,description}`); `save` finds the most-recent 0xF2 frame's (segment,offset); `restore` resolves the label + delegates to the existing `rollback apply --confirm` path (zero new restore logic). 11 tests (valid/empty/too-long/leading-dot+traversal/slash+space+backslash + index round-trip/absent/atomic-no-tmp + find-latest-snapshot none/picks-last). default test exit 0 + clippy 0 + docgen for `neoth checkpoint`.
- [x] **SC-03** Plugin signature verification + revocation list. — 3d — **Integrity gate (SHA-256 pinning) shipped 2026-05-28 Session 28b** (`cb2e1c9`) — panel teed-up move #1, security-reviewer-confirmed SOUND. The WASM loader handed plugin.wasm to wasmtime with ZERO integrity check; an attacker writing `~/.neoth/plugins/<id>/` could swap the binary. New `discovery::verify_integrity` + `IntegrityPolicy` gate at instantiation (serve.rs bootstrap retain, BEFORE wasmtime compile — no TOCTOU, bytes read+hashed once): pin-match → run / mismatch → HashMismatch refuse / unpinned+require_all_pinned → refuse / unpinned default → run (opt-in-secure, non-breaking). `content_hash` on every DiscoveredPlugin, surfaced by `neoth plugin list` (+ `--output json` full sha256) so the operator knows what to pin in `freedom.yaml::plugins.wasm.pinned_hashes`. + inactive-gate warn when Active plugins run unpinned. 5 discovery tests; full suite 5913 passed; clippy clean (default + wasm-plugin-host). **Scoped decision:** SHA-256 content-pinning (no new crypto dep, mirrors skills `content_hash`/ARCH-07) fully closes the tamper/swap threat. **Remaining for full [x]:** asymmetric ed25519 SIGNATURE authenticity (sign new plugins without re-pinning) + a revocation list — the heavier crypto half, deferred (needs ed25519-dalek dep + a `neoth plugin sign`/keygen UX). **SIGNATURE-AUTHENTICITY + REVOCATION SHIPPED 2026-05-31 Session 31 (4-lens senior-dev gremium runner-up pick, 3/4 lenses #2).** Verify-first corrected the original "needs ed25519-dalek" framing: `minisign-verify 0.2` is ALREADY a compiled dep (added for MV-01b self-update) and exposes the full verify surface, so the authenticity gate ships with ZERO new deps — only the SIGNING side (producing signatures) needs a private key, and operators already have the standard `minisign -Sm plugin.wasm` tool for that, so a NEOTH-native `sign`/`keygen` wrapper is an unneeded convenience (ed25519-dalek stays out of the tree). **Code:** `DiscoveredPlugin` gained `signature: Option<String>` (read from the `plugin.wasm.minisig` companion in `load_one`); `IntegrityPolicy` gained `author_pubkey: Option<&str>` + `require_signature: bool` + `revoked: &[String]` (borrows keep it `Copy`); new `verify_plugin_signature(data, signature, pubkey_b64, require)` mirrors `updater/sig_verify.rs::check_signature` EXACTLY but takes a RUNTIME operator key (`freedom.yaml::plugins.wasm.author_pubkey`) instead of the compile-time release key — so an operator can trust a third-party plugin author without rebuilding NEOTH. `verify_integrity` restructured into a fail-closed 3-stage gate: **(1) revocation** (id in `revoked_ids` → `Revoked`, the kill switch checked FIRST) → **(2) SHA-256 pin** (existing tamper/swap) → **(3) author signature** (valid `.minisig` → pass; missing + `require_signature` → `SignatureMissing`; invalid/wrong-key/tamper → `SignatureInvalid`, ALWAYS refused regardless of `require`). Two-tier gate: no `author_pubkey` → checking off (back-compat, existing plugins load); `author_pubkey` set → present-but-invalid sig always refused, missing sig refused only under `require_signature`. The pubkey + revocation list come ONLY from operator config (never from the plugin → no attacker-chosen-key vector). New `config::WasmPluginsConfig.{author_pubkey, require_signature, revoked_ids}` (all `#[serde(default)]` → freedom.yaml back-compat). `cli/serve.rs` daemon plugin-bootstrap now threads all three into the per-Active-plugin `verify_integrity` gate. New **`neoth plugin verify <path>`** (`run_verify`) applies the SAME `freedom.yaml` policy + the SAME `verify_integrity` gate without instantiating — so the CLI verdict and the daemon's load-time refusal can never disagree; exits non-zero on FAIL for CI/pre-install gating; Table + JSON. **Latent feature-build fix folded in (solve-roadblocks-now):** the `wasm-plugin-host` build was ALREADY RED on HEAD (proven via stash — UX-07's `run_test_invoke` called the wasmtime API wrong: `Module` vs `Arc<Module>`, `&NeothEngine` vs `engine.raw()`, un-unwrapped `build_linker` Result); fixed so SC-03 could be verified under the feature. 8 new tests (revocation-rejected-first / no-key-passes / sig-missing-under-require / present-but-invalid-rejected / `verify_plugin_signature` two-tier matrix at the discovery layer + 2 `run_verify` CLI arg-guards) — the Verified happy-path needs a real keypair (documented limitation, same as `sig_verify.rs`). Verified: clippy `--lib --tests` 0 warnings on BOTH the default AND `--features wasm-plugin-host` builds, default lib **6256/0**, feature build 90 wasm_plugin + 11 cli::plugin tests green, fmt clean. **STILL OPEN (pure convenience, not a gate gap):** a NEOTH-native `neoth plugin sign`/`keygen` wrapper around `minisign` (operators sign with the standard tool today). **Adversarial-review hardening (4-lens security review → 22 raw → 13 confirmed / 5 refuted, all confirmed fixed before the gate shipped — the core crypto was confirmed SOUND: revocation-first ordering, present-but-invalid always refuses, allow_legacy=false, pubkey sourced only from config):** **3 HIGH** — (1) the `.minisig` companion was read with an UNBOUNDED `fs::read_to_string` BEFORE any manifest/activation filter → a hostile multi-GB companion in the attacker-controlled plugin dir would OOM the daemon at startup; now a shared `read_capped_minisig` (4 KiB cap) refuses over-size companions; (2) `neoth plugin verify` used `unwrap_or_default()` on the config → a CORRUPT freedom.yaml silently became an empty all-gates-OFF policy → false PASS for revoked/tampered/unsigned plugins (the daemon takes the OPPOSITE default — all-Pending); now it BAILS on a load error (a MISSING file still yields the correct open default since `load_from_default_path` returns `Ok(default)` on absence); (3) the Table output printed "signature verified" whenever a key was configured — even on a FAIL verdict — now the author-key line reflects the actual verdict (verified / check FAILED / unsigned). **2 MED security** — symlinks in the plugin root are now refused (`is_dir()` follows symlinks → an attacker could alias `<id>/` to an arbitrary path, sourcing `plugin.wasm` bytes + the giant-`.minisig` from the target, dir-name attacker-chosen); a `warn!` now fires when `author_pubkey` is set + `require_signature=false` + a plugin ships no signature (silent unsigned-load — the operator who set a key isn't lulled). **2 MED correctness** — `require_signature=true` with no `author_pubkey` now maps to a distinct `AuthorKeyNotConfigured` error (was `SignatureInvalid` → wrong remediation); `neoth plugin verify` now reads manifest+wasm+sig DIRECTLY (bypassing the id==dirname locality check) so it works on a CI checkout whose dir name ≠ plugin id (the daemon load path keeps the locality check; verify is a pre-install INTEGRITY gate). **2 LOW + nits** — legacy non-prehashed signatures get an actionable "re-sign with `minisign -Sm`" message instead of a key-mismatch red herring; `sig_text` trimmed (symmetry with the pubkey trim); serve.rs gate-warn now names revocation+signature, not just hash-pin; `eq_ignore_ascii_case` documented (tolerates operator-pasted uppercase pins, intentional). 2 new tests (config-error mapping + capped-read). Re-verified: clippy `--lib --tests` 0 warnings BOTH builds, default lib **6258/0**, feature 92 wasm_plugin + 11 cli::plugin green, fmt clean.

- [x] **SC-04b** WASM plugin hostcall **runtime capability gate** — the load-bearing enforcement behind the README "WASM plugin capability sandbox: Yes" / "No ambient plugin power" claim. — **SHIPPED 2026-05-31 Session 32 (Alex-flagged false-guarantee → 3-lens design gremium → build → 4-lens adversarial bypass-hunt).** **The hole:** `hostcalls.rs`'s doc claimed "each hostcall is gated by a `PermissionToken<L>`" but `PermissionToken` existed ONLY in that comment — `build_linker` got the bare engine, no manifest/permission threaded, `PluginStoreState` carried no granted-level field, and `manifest.requested_permissions` was read NOWHERE at runtime. So EVERY loaded plugin got `log`+`fuel_left`+`emit_event` (WAL **write**)+`recall_top` (DB **read**) regardless of what its `plugin.toml` declared — ambient authority, and the README sold the opposite. **Fix (wasmtime-idiomatic — gate via Store-state, not the shared Linker):** `PluginStoreState` gained `granted: HostcallPermission` (DEFAULT `None` — fail-closed) + `with_granted`; `HostcallPermission` gained a monotonic `level()` ladder (None0<ReadOnly1<Write2<Execute3<Dangerous4) + `allows(required)=self.level()>=required.level()` + `From<RequestedPermission>` (1:1). Each privileged closure now reads `caller.data().granted` and refuses fail-closed BEFORE any effect: `emit_event` (needs Write) → no WAL write + return code **7**; `recall_top` (needs ReadOnly) → return 0 hits. `log`/`fuel_left` are permission-`None` (never gated). **Audit:** new `EVENT_TYPE_PLUGIN_CAP_DENIED = 0xC7` (tool/plugin band, band-assert + name-registry + uniqueness + pin + **durable/immediate-sync** + SC-01a emit-site guard) emitted on every denial — a plugin reaching beyond its grant is VISIBLE in `neoth wal show --type plugin_cap_denied`, never silent. Also reconciled the latent **0xC6 doc/code mismatch** Alex flagged (doc said "batchable"; code fsyncs it) → both cap-audit frames are durable, doc corrected + drift-pinned. **Threading:** `invoke_plugin` gained `granted` + `wal_writer` + `recall_db` params; `CompiledPluginInvoker` carries `grants: HashMap<id,perm>` (built from `manifest.requested_permissions` via `grants_from_report`, keyed by `manifest.id` same as `modules`, `unwrap_or(None)` = fail-closed) + `with_runtime_handles`. **The adversarial-review HIGH (multi-lens converged):** the production hook path built the store with NO writer → `0xC7`/`0xC4`/`0xC6` were NEVER emitted in prod → the audit claim was HOLLOW (also pre-existing for KF-09's 0xC6). FIXED: `bootstrap_plugin_invoker` moved AFTER the WAL writer spawns + threads a CLONE of the daemon's single writer (not a 2nd writer — preserves the single-writer segment invariant) + opens `views.db` read-only for `recall_db`. **Informed consent:** `neoth plugin enable` now discloses the capability level being granted ("may READ your memory AND WRITE audit frames to your WAL") + that over-level calls are refused & audited. **Tests:** real-WASM gate proof — hand-encoded `calling_recall_wasm`/`calling_emit_wasm` modules that actually CALL the hostcall at runtime, asserting None-grant→deny (0/7) vs granted→allow (1/0) on the SAME db (gate is the controlling variable); `allows()` ladder + `From` + `0xC7` payload units; `enable` disclosure unit; AND an **end-to-end `CompiledPluginInvoker::invoke` → real 0xC7-frame-on-disk** test closing the review's coverage finding. default lib **6303/0**, feature wasm-plugin-host **6370/0** (1 pre-existing HLC-timing flake passes isolated), SC-01a + uniqueness green, clippy 0 warnings BOTH builds, fmt clean. **STILL OPEN (tracked, Phase-2 — NOT a hole in the current claim):** operator granting LESS than the manifest requests (a persisted per-plugin `granted_level` distinct from the manifest ceiling + an enable-time level picker) — today grant == manifest level, which the operator approves by enabling (standard install-time permission model + signature/integrity + default-inactive); the runtime gate makes "No ambient plugin power" defensible. The least-privilege refinement is SC-04c.

The `enforce_skill_allowlist` gate (shipped 28b, 2 tests) is now reached on the channel path with the real allowlist. 4 new tests (`cli::serve::tests`): None-when-no-match / Some(empty)-for-default-manifest / carries-restrictive-list / route-then-allowlist-preserves-restriction-end-to-end (composes the real `route()` + the helper to prove a restrictive allowlist survives — the exact regression guard against re-hardcoding `None`). New `security::credential_redact` module with typed-wrapper gate (same SC-16 `PaperlessOcrPayload` pattern). `CredentialImportRecord { source, entries: Vec<CredentialEntry { name, url, username, secret, tags }>, target_vault_id, ts_unix }` is the wizard-side rich type; `RedactedCredentialImportPayload { source, entry_count, distinct_tags_sorted, services_hash (SHA-256-truncated to 16 hex chars over sorted-unique-lowercased names), target_vault_id, ts_unix, services_redacted: true }` is the WAL-side payload — every field intentionally non-identifying. **The ONLY public path from Record to Payload is `redact_credential_import(&record)`** — no public `Payload` constructor; future WAL writer that tries to stuff a `username` into the payload fails review on the first PR (typed wrapper makes the bypass unrepresentable). `ImportSource { Bitwarden, Keepass, OnePassword, SystemKeyring, WizardPrompt }` snake_case + `as_str()` audit pin. `services_hash(&names) -> String` is sort+dedup+lowercase+SHA-256→8-byte→16hex; deterministic + order-independent + case-insensitive → same hash for repeated imports of the same set (auditor proves "this import ran twice" without learning the service names) but distinct names → distinct hashes. `distinct_tags_sorted` aggregates tags across entries with sort+dedup so the count can't be inferred per-tag. 20 tests: source as_str + snake_case serde pins / services_hash deterministic + order-independent + case-insensitive + drops-duplicates + 16-hex-chars + empty-names-still-hashes + differs-on-distinct-inputs / redactor drops name+url+username+secret (asserts via JSON-grep none of the leaked strings appear ANYWHERE in the serialised output) / redactor keeps entry_count / distinct_tags_sorted aggregated + sorted / services_hash proves repeated imports (case-different inputs hash same) / services_hash differs on distinct imports / target_vault_id verbatim / source+ts preserved / services_redacted always true / empty record still valid payload / json pinned field names (drift guard for auditor grep) / unique tag dedup within one entry. 20/20 security::credential_redact suite green.
